## PENDING.md's Done section, by release (r55 back)

#### Closed 2026-10-27, at r64's first commit (built, still listed as open; the r63 audit, A-11)
303 was finished by r63 (the legacy script deleted, every line in the typed modules); 305 and 308 were built in r56.1 (PENDING 332, `tests/r56-1.js`); 306, 307 and 309 in r63 (PENDING 358 B, `tests/r63.js` section 3). Their texts as they stood:

303. Building (V 2026-10-16: "use real languages", "pristine architecting", "don't hardcode", asked many times and not done; V's go the same day): Ironlog moves from one 10,000-line script in one scope to TypeScript modules bundled by esbuild, one phase per pull request, each proved to change nothing a lifter sees. The plan, the rules and the measured size of what is left are in ARCHITECTURE.md; tests/hygiene.js enforces the rules, so they do not depend on anyone remembering them. Phase 1 is built in r49: the toolchain (esbuild and TypeScript, strict, checked on every run; the old code bundled unchanged inside its original wrapper, so its const and let behave as written); src/app/config.ts as the one place for the app name, the preview name and label, the wordmark, the version people see (2.0) and the build behind it, the site address, the export file prefix, and the storage id from which every storage key, cache name and backup mark is built; 33 typed names in the screens, the page title and heading, the manifest, the service worker and the account script now read it, and so do the storage keys, the backup mark and the export file names (one of which, the preview's copy of the live log, was found typed by hand by the new check). The menu says Version 2.0; Diagnostics, Copy diagnostics, the recovery screen, the preview's Settings and problem reports say the version and the build. Checks that fail on the name, the address, a build id or the storage id typed anywhere else, on a module importing outside src/app/ (any import form, any depth), on a config name bound again anywhere, on a type error, and on the old code growing in declarations or lines (the ratchet: 928 and 10,452, which may only go down); scripts/depmap.js measures it. The bundled page keeps only some of the source's comments, so the suites that read the page's text were moved to what they meant to check: r43 builds its simulated next build from changed source through scripts/join.js, r29 reads the body's shape from the app, update reads the build from the app-build tag, r29c and r34b scan the source files with their comments, r29b checks every inline script (it had only ever read the library loader), and r27 and preview check the version instead of the build. An independent review before release found the one suite that broke on the bundled page (r43), five gaps in the new checks, and stale docs; each is fixed, and each gap was shown caught by planting the mistake. Phase 2 is built in r50 (V's go 2026-10-16, on a branch after r49): 109 declarations that read nothing but their arguments, each other and (uid and today) the clock moved into src/app/util.ts, units.ts, library.ts, routines.ts and formulas.ts, typed (src/app/types.ts holds the record shapes they read; each library row's main muscle is checked against the muscle keys, and the weekly bands must cover every muscle), with their comments, and deleted from the old code in the same change (928 to 819 declarations, 10,452 to 9,863 lines). tests/moved.js proves the move changed no code: each moved declaration against r49's, after esbuild's printer, so types and layout are free and a one-value change fails (shown on a library row and a routine's rest), and every name in it must point where it did in r49. tests/hygiene.js now fails on moved code existing twice, on a moved name the old code uses but does not import, on an import nothing uses, and on any name the old code reads that is not a known browser or JavaScript global (a missing import would otherwise break only when its line ran). An independent review before release found the comparison of text alone could be passed by five changes that alter behaviour (a renamed import, a private helper, a statement run on load, a reassigned export, a TypeScript declare), a deleted name passing every check, the modules running in sloppy mode inside the bundle, two type guards and four comments claiming more than the code does, and stale lines in the docs; each is fixed, and each check was shown failing on the planted mistake. The version people see becomes 2.1. Phase 3a is built in r51 (V 2026-10-17: "start on the plan and work"; the plan is in ARCHITECTURE.md, Phase 3a in detail), on a branch on top of r50: 176 more self-contained declarations moved into 18 new modules and four of r50's, each where it belongs in the finished design (four of r50's modules grew), and 39 left on purpose with the reason for each (screen markup and `howToUrl` wait for phase 5, analytics steps for 3b, the undo constants and the log scrubber for phase 4, `EMAIL_RE` for the account sheets, and the retired Claude stubs for item 304); `src/app/store.ts` owns `state`, `ui`, `cloud`, `acct` and `acctWait`, and the 19 places that replace the whole log go through `setState`. 638 declarations and 9,134 lines are left in the old code. An independent review before release found no change in behaviour and two holes in the proof (a module imported only to run it, and code added on purpose held only by a light check); both now fail, as do the reviewer's own attacks, and seven misplaced comments went back beside their code. A second independent review, on those fixes, again found no change in behaviour and four more holes (code run from the two hand-written modules, a re-exported name, an import circle that left History's page size undefined, a copy of the old code read in place of the real one); each is closed by a new check, and all thirteen of the reviewers' attacks now fail. The modules now have layers that may depend only downward, with no import circles. A mutation test then found 15 more ways, from five causes (ARCHITECTURE.md, Built (r51)): the checks read src/app/ by file name one level deep, nothing compared the module code on the page with the modules, a destructured binding was not a declaration, the legacy locator took the last match, and config, tsconfig and the config loader were unguarded; each cause is closed and all 51 attacks replayed to a failure. The release's own independent review then found an assertion deleted by accident (every import used; restored), and four unguarded places now guarded: the page around the app script is held to the baseline's, what scripts/build.js adds to the shipped page is pinned by hash (tests/dist-page.json), every baseline is pinned by hash (baselines/SHA256SUMS), and the workflow runs the two proof suites on their own (one since r54, which retired tests/moved.js); its six probes replayed to a failure. The modules were then given real types in place of about 200 casts (each cast left is one TypeScript needs, explained beside it), a comment on every export (58 had none; tests/hygiene.js now requires one), one formatter for every module, and the layer list in one place. V (2026-10-17): no shortcuts, no workarounds, no exceptions; CLAUDE.md rule 9. tests/moved.js now also holds the old code left behind to r50's, statement for statement in order once `setState(x)` is read back as `state = x`, compares with whitespace minified, and runs an executable check for anything a phase adds or changes on purpose. Then the outside review V brought (2026-10-17; ARCHITECTURE.md, The outside review), each point checked in the code: r51 adds four checks, each shown failing on planted mistakes: no value typed any (by the type checker), every cast and `!` with a comment naming it (18 of 61 had no stated reason, though r51 had said each did) and no cast to a type parameter, the unchecked forms (type predicates, hand-typed callback parameters) counted as casts, their counts ratcheted (76 casts, 25 `!`, 18 open types, after three reviews restated them); every catch in a module carrying a comment; outside addresses, library versions, the font faces and weights defined once in config.ts (the password check's origin had been typed twice, the libraries' addresses three or four times, the fonts four) and read by the source, the scripts and the test fixtures, with any other outside address listed with its phase; and tests/cleanup.js, holding charts, drag lists, intervals and WebGL contexts to what is on screen. An independent review of those checks found a hole in each, and a second and a third review of the fixes found more (ARCHITECTURE.md lists all three), all closed and replayed to a failure; three comments and three backlog claims that were wrong are corrected, and the tab bar's account was rebuilt twice from measured page heights; the first review also found item 308. The points that change running code are 305 to 307. Also in r51 (two iPhone users, 2026-10-17: in the home-screen app the tab bar rises and the page shrinks just tapping through the tabs, on History, Settings and sometimes Plan; never on V's phone, which is not on iOS 26): iOS 26 gives an installed app's page shorter than the screen a viewport shorter by the status bar's height, so the fixed bar stops above the bottom edge (frappe-ui pull requests 1284 and 1289). With a real log Plan (about 780 px) is shorter than every current iPhone and Today (860 to 920) than the larger ones, while History and Settings are taller (ARCHITECTURE.md, The outside review, item 5); Plan comes before Stats, History and Settings in the bar, and Stats (about 1,050 px) is taller too, so the reading that fits is a short viewport begun on Plan that carries over. The installed app now holds every page to at least the screen's height, capped by 100lvh so nothing changes where iOS has no such bug; tests/ios-bar.js and tests/moved.js (r50's page plus exactly that rule, at its anchor) hold it. Two iPhone users confirmed the bar now stays down (V, 2026-10-17 20:48, after r51 went live). The version people see becomes 2.2. Phase 3b is built in r52 (V's go 2026-10-17 20:57; the plan, written before any code and reviewed twice, is in ARCHITECTURE.md, Phase 3b in detail, and what building found is under Built (r52)): the load engine and the analytics index, 120 declarations, moved into seven new modules and five grown ones: the 97 that read the log, the date or a cache are functions of a model (the log, the date read as today, the caches), taking it as their first parameter `M` and named with `In`, 16 moved unchanged, and the 7 caches are the model's, given by `freshCaches`; `live.ts` holds the one model the app runs on and 74 adapters, so the screens call them under their old names and not one of their calls changed. 518 declarations and 7,585 lines are left in the old code. Proof: tests/moved.js reads each function taking the model back to r51's code on the syntax tree and compares it token for token (97, with the 16 moved unchanged), refusing any other use of the model, and holds the nine prunes TypeScript needed, the four new declarations, the adapters, the caches retired and the hook's 22 new names to fixed lists and checks; tests/hygiene.js's rule 6 holds every function taking the model, and all it reaches, to reading nothing but the model (no clock, randomness, store, page or changing module value); tests/r52.js compares r51 and r52 on 1,000 wide histories (1.63 million calls, all equal), past midnight in a page left open, with each cache stale on purpose and on the demo; tests/engine.js runs the engine alone in Node on the page's frozen logs (1.63 million calls on two fresh models each, all equal to the page) with its lines run as a ratchet. 55 planted mistakes were each caught, 17 of them from two independent reviews, which found no change in behaviour but checks weaker than they said, each now closed; the one difference a lifter could see is the wording of the crash screen when code that moved throws (`IDXIn(...)` for `IDX(...)`), stated in ARCHITECTURE.md. The version people see becomes 2.3. Phase 4 (storage and sync, alone) and the screens (two tabs a release, the session screen last and alone) follow, four releases from r52, each waiting for the one before to merge and for V's go.
305. Phase 4 (from the outside review V brought, 2026-10-17; ARCHITECTURE.md, The outside review): one storage module reads every stored value, each key through a typed check of every entry. Today `normalize()` checks the log on every load and the demo settings kept aside (`demoRestore`), but the sync bookkeeping (`cloud.meta`, `cloud.editAt`, `cloud.last`, read at start and from another tab's storage event) is checked only at the top level, so a damaged entry reaches the merge's base as it is; the sign-up marker's time is read as it is; and the error log's entries (`logBuf`) are not checked at all. `tests/hygiene.js` will then fail on `localStorage` or a parse of stored text outside that module. `log` moves in the same phase, and the catch rule tightens to: log it, or say why it is expected.
306. With the menu's sheets in phase 5, and one SQL step for V when it is built (from the same review): a problem report has no time limit, so a request that never answers leaves the sheet on Sending with the button disabled, and no key made on the phone, so a report that reached the table but whose reply was lost is sent again by a retry, making a second report and, through feedback-to-issue, a second ticket. Read in `ACT.fbSend` and `ironlogFeedback.send`; not reproduced (the build machine cannot reach Supabase). The fix: the time limit the sync already uses (`withTimeout`), and a `client_id` made on the phone with a unique column in `public.feedback`, the insert ignoring a repeat (OWNER.md gets the SQL then).
307. Phases 4 and 5 (from the same review): the sync status becomes one typed set of states with one function that moves between them; the account flow (sign in, create, waiting for the email, the code) becomes one typed set of states in place of `acctWait.busy`, `acctWait.coding` and its timer, which can contradict each other; the account errors' `code` values (bad_code, breached, denied, error, limit, no_code, not_deployed, not_set_up, revoked, signin and wait, in `scripts/cloud-supabase.js`) become one typed list, and the sync's own codes (timeout, too_big) another; the open types in `src/app/` (index signatures and `Record<string, unknown>`, 18 today, most in `src/app/store.ts`) close as their writers move (counted by the ratchet in ARCHITECTURE.md, so the number only falls).
308. Found by the r51 review (2026-10-17), a change for a release V chooses: a session's start time `t` is kept by `normalize()` if it is any positive number, so a damaged or hand-edited save with a time out of range (1e20, say) shows "Invalid Date" beside that session in History (`sessTm`, `src/app/format.ts`; checked in Node: the date formats to "Invalid Date" and nothing throws). Only a crafted or broken save can do it; the app never writes such a time. The fix is `normalize()` keeping only a time a Date can hold, a change to the stored-data rules, so it goes in phase 4 with the storage code (and `tests/migration.js` shows nothing real is dropped).
- 309, with 306 and 307: a sign-in request that never answers leaves the sign-in sheet on Signing in, with no time limit, as a problem report does (306); the same fix as 306 (the time limit the sync already uses, `withTimeout`), with the account flow in phase 5.

#### r55, live since 2026-10-20 (pull request #48, merged by V; build 2026.10.19-r55)
Version 2.6: V's four answers (323), the journey audit of r54 (324), two independent reviews (326, 328) and an end-to-end audit (327); frozen by V on 2026-10-20 so that findings older than r55 went to r55.1 and r56. 327 and 328 below are the full texts; their open parts are in PENDING.md.

323. V on the r54 preview (2026-10-19), each reproduced on r54 with the real buttons before it was put to V, and V's answers the same day, all four the option put first: (a) a set typed as 0 reps and ticked became last time's reps (100 x 0 ticked saved as 100 x 6, the grey number), and left unticked it was dropped at Finish: V chose to log it as missed, kept and shown ("300 x 0, missed") and counted toward no volume, PR, estimate or target, the next set's grey numbers coming from the last set completed; (b) after two sets of 50 x 6 and a set of 300 x 1 on the last row, + Set copied 300 x 1 (the adjustment that lowers the load after a set well under the range runs only while a planned row is still to come, so a 4-set bench lowered it and a 3-set row did not): + Set takes the same adjustment; and before a load far off the session's sets (300 after sets of 50), V chose to ask first, Log it or Fix it; (c) Settings > More kept Load demo data beside Clear demo data once demo data was loaded, and Diagnostics showed lines meant for the owner: V chose one demo button (Load, then Clear once loaded) and the version only, the rest of Diagnostics only when an error is logged; (d) 319: a lift trained in two rep ranges reads falling only when it falls in every range in use, as stalls do since r54.
324. The journey audit of r54 (2026-10-19, four audits run by lifter journey: logging, the sessions after, the weeks and Stats and Plan, and data and accounts), each finding reproduced on r54 with the real buttons before it was fixed, each fix with a case in `tests/r55.js` or `tests/r55b.js` that fails on r54. Logging: a decimal load typed while the exercise above folded became 1525 (the field keeps its text across the redraw); a ticked set's reps cleared were ticked again at the grey reps (now unticked); a mistaken tick undone kept the grey numbers typed and the exercise where auto-reorder moved it (both put back within 30 s); reps put off once were saved at Finish's Mark it done without the question (now asked there too); two sets removed with − Set and both beaten read Beaten while the target repeated (now "Fewer sets than usual: the target stays"); Update the day after a swap gave the new lift the old one's plan (now its own); a lighter day's header said the plan's effort (now 3+ RIR, in the lifter's scale); a swap of an exercise with every set logged added the new one with an empty set (now asks: move the sets, or add it); warm-ups typed and left unticked were dropped at Finish without the question the Guide promised; a warm-up with no load was called a hard set; a 150 s hold was questioned as 150 reps; 8.5 reps showed 8.5 and stored 9 (now the field shows what is stored). The sessions after: a session stopped short at another load greyed its reps at the load the target left; after a change of units a set read as a new record; the Guide's bad-day lines no longer matched the rule; an extra set past the plan's sets held the step back; a rep target past the top of the range, then a recap saying 0 of 1 beaten before a step; weighted pull-ups with no weigh-in stepped against the added load alone; an assisted lift at no help was asked to take help off; a past workout's Heaviest line showed a later set; the PR list named a different set than the toast; a hold never read back after a break. The weeks, Stats and Plan: Plan's header planned 46 sets a week beside Today's 39 on Strength 5 x 5; a rest week's help named Settings, and its week bar said planned and missed; New blank made three training days and no rest, and a rest day given an exercise kept the name Rest; the muscle map counted a 0 minimum with nothing logged as off target beside "Every muscle at target"; changing or deleting the routine trained on rescored past weeks (now each session records what its routine planned a week, `pw`: sessions saved before r55 have no record and still read the routine as it is now, or the active one once it is deleted; recording them now would rewrite every saved session, which is phase 4's to weigh with the sync, item 307); bests brought in from June read the weeks between as missed (now the weeks count from after a break of more than 4 weeks, as a lift's targets do); a bodyweight lift with no weigh-in showed its first session as the best set and asked for 3 sessions after 8, and (found while fixing that) weighted pull-ups with no weigh-in read 40% stronger over three weeks from the added load alone, 5% at an 80 kg weigh-in (now a bodyweight lift's trend needs a weigh-in, as its goal has since r40); on the RPE scale Plan's effort field took RIR under an RPE line; muscles inside day-to-day noise read "too early to tell" after 10 weeks; a range changed in Plan never reached the lift in a blank session. Data and accounts: a new phone's first-run workouts, draft and weigh-in were replaced at a first sign-in, the merge offered an Undo that deleted the account's sessions on every device, and demo data went into the account; after Erase everything the Undo was out of reach; the rest timer ran on with no session; Back closed a sheet with typing in it; the app's own CSV lost cardio, notes and minutes on import; the update bar covered the first-run page; section summaries were cut at 320 px; Settings' switches were 20 px tall and Plan's day names 40; Build my own kept the untouched starter; Your training set in the first-run picker did not reach the routine picked (now applied as its Apply does, said in the toast); a report opened signed out was gone after signing in from it; the Guide said every first-run import makes a routine. Not changed, and why: the Consistency squares and the month calendar are 38 px at 320 px (seven to a row cannot reach 44 there; from 375 px they are 44 and more, as the week bar's days have been since r23).
326. The independent review of r55 (2026-10-19, on commit 54d781e against r54, briefed to report correctness and data loss only, each with a reproduction it ran) found five, each reproduced again before it was fixed, each fix with a case in `tests/r55.js` or `tests/r55b.js`: (1) a workout in progress on two phones at a first sign-in, one of the two lost on every device (r54 too): the phone signing in keeps its own in progress and the other's ticked sets are saved in History (`draftSession`, Finish's own way of saving a block, `savedEx`, moved out of Finish unchanged: `tests/r55.js` holds it to r54's own line, byte for byte on 3,000 random exercises), and the toast says so; (2) a routine built on a new phone with nothing logged was replaced by the account's copy at a first sign-in (r54 too): a routine or custom exercise made on the phone now counts as its own, so the two are merged; (3) demo data went into an empty account at a first sign-in (r54 too): it is cleared, as a merge already did (`stripDemo`, the one way demo data leaves a log); (4) new in r55: a lift falling in one range with a second range trained once was said to be "not" down in it, with no reading there: the line says that range has too few sessions to tell (`quietRR`), and the lift is still not read as falling, as for a stall; (5) new in r55: a comment added to Build my own's line hid two statements after it, so the Days section opened folded and a day picked on Today stayed picked: fixed, and `tests/hygiene.js` now fails on any comment at the end of a legacy line that hides code. Not exercised by the review: WebKit, Undo of Move the sets after a rebuild, and auto-reorder's undo with other exercises moved between.
327. The end-to-end audit of r55 (2026-10-19 and 20, by lifter journey, after the independent review of 326), each finding reproduced with the real buttons on r55 and on r54. Part one, data and accounts, fixed in r55 (commit 9a17d75), each case in `tests/r55.js` or `tests/r55b.js` failing on the build before it: (1) new in r55: signing back in after Sign out with Keep on this phone treated the phone as a new one, so its old copy undid edits and brought back sessions and weigh-ins deleted on other phones since; Sign out with Keep now leaves the sync notes with the copy (`cloudStop(forget)`, forgetting them only on Delete account, Sign out and remove, or Erase everything signed out), so signing back in merges from where the phone left off, as a sync does, and a workout logged while signed out is kept (r54 dropped it); (2) a second tape save the same day replaced the day's entry: it adds to it now, keeping what it does not retype, with Undo (`addMeas`); (3) reps typed, then that set's load tapped and typed: the set ticked under the typing and stored 101050 (20000 lb); now it ticks when the load is left (`pendAuto` from the load field), and a ticked set's load changed to one far off asks Log it or Fix it, as at the tick; (4) pasted notes read a unit written on a line under an earlier line's unit (100kg as 100 lb): a line's own unit is that line's (`impTextSets`); (5) the import preview said km for distances shown in miles. Found and not built in r55, by V's plan of 2026-10-20 (r55 frozen): one workout open on two phones at once, both ticking sets, loses one phone's sets (for r56; the audit's script was not kept, so r56 writes it again from this: two phones signed in to one account, one session in progress on both, a set ticked on each, then both sync), and the three V's decisions put in r55.1 (325 a, imports' notes and durations, the deleted account's message). Part two, the session tools, Your gym, PRs, Undo, the effort scales and 20 simulated weeks (2026-10-20, an independent auditor), for r55.1, each present on r54 as well, so none new in r55; each is to be reproduced again with a test that fails before its fix: (a) Type or dictate sets marks its sets done without the far-off question (`qFill`, `fillSets` do not go through `setDone`): `1000x8, 100x8, 100x8` on a bench last done at 100 x 8 x 4 is saved without asking and Finish records a Weight PR of 1000 x 8 (best estimate 1285.5 kg); `100x80, 8, 8` a Rep PR of 100 x 80; (b) a change of units makes a weight PR: bench at 100 kg x 8 x 4 twice, Units set to lb, the Last column showing 220.5 x 8, and 220.5 x 8 typed and ticked: a live Weight PR toast, the recap's PR, best estimate 284 lb and Heaviest dated today, since 220.5 lb is 100.017 kg and `prSameLoad` reads a load back to the old one only on a 5 or 2.5 lb spacing (the Invariants: a change of units is never a PR); (c) Your gym's plates are not kept to on a barbell lift last logged off the new plate spacing: bench at 230 lb, the 2.5 lb plates turned off (45 + 10n loadable): next 240, 230 or 200, a lighter day 200 or 180, warm-ups 90, 140, 130, 80 and 120, while the plate line itself says 2.5 lb not loadable; in kg without 1.25 kg plates after 102.5: next 107.5, 102.5 or 92.5, a lighter day or deload 87.5 or 82.5; a kg log read in lb with no 2.5 lb plates: bench 220, squat 310, lighter days 190 and 260; dumbbells snap to the rack (`rackSnap`), plates do not; (d) a lift in two rep ranges back after more than 4 weeks: Day A bench 5 x 3-5 and Day B bench 4 x 8-12 to Jul 17, six weeks off, Day B on Aug 24 eased to about 90%, then Day A on Aug 26 asks for the full 85 kg and 25+ reps over 5 sets, with no easing, against the Guide's restart rule (`backDaysIn` reads the newest session of any range, so the eased light day ends the heavy day's return too); (e) Undo after Make it lighter (the check-in) says Undone but the exercises keep their halved sets, lighter loads and lighter mark; only the note goes back (the copy kept for Undo is normalized, giving `plan` new keys and `swappedFrom: null`, so `mergeDraft` reads every exercise as changed since and keeps it). Checked by the auditor and found right: Your gym racks in kg and lb for targets, lighter days, deloads, adjustments, drops and warm-ups; a PR on a lighter or deload day; History edits, deletes, Undo and Restore; Undo of remove exercise, Short on time, − Set, Your gym, goals, rest days, weigh-ins and sessions; the RPE and half-point scales from the strip to the CSV; missed sets and sets left undone at Finish; 20 simulated weeks of stalls, trends, streaks and missed weeks. Not settled: a missed set plus + Set on a load target reads 3 of 4 sets hit on screen and 0 of 1 beaten in the recap. Not checked: WebKit, touch drags, Discard, reload mid-session, supersets, past workouts, Corrected RIR, Limited equipment, an injury's Undo, imports and sync.
328. The independent review of r55's later code (2026-10-20, everything after 54d781e, against r54, briefed to report correctness and data loss only, each finding with a reproduction it ran on both builds). Fixed in r55 (V's plan of 2026-10-20: a problem found in r55's own new code is fixed in r55), each case in `tests/r55.js`, `tests/r55b.js` or `tests/preview.js` failing on the build reviewed (3825898): (1) new in r55, data loss: a phone signed out keeping a copy, a backup restored on it, then signed back in: the restored log was merged against the sync notes kept with the old copy, so the account's sessions the backup did not hold were deleted from the account and every phone, unannounced (r54 kept them all); now restoring a backup, Copy my log in and Load demo data while signed out forget the notes, as Erase everything already did (`cloudStop(true)`), so the next sign-in is a first link, which removes nothing; (2) demo data loaded while signed out keeping a copy went into the account at the next sign-in (27 sessions, 28 weigh-ins; r54 the same once the app was reopened): settled by the same rule, a first link leaving demo data out; (3) the other device's workout kept at a first sign-in lost its check-in, its start and its cardio (r54 lost the whole workout): kept now as Finish keeps them (`draftSession`, `draftCardio`, one reading of a workout's cardio for both); (4) when that device then ticked a set before it pulled and finished, its History held the workout twice under one id until a reload (5 sets for 3, the extra copy later in Recently deleted): Finish puts a session and its cardio in by id (`putById`), so a copy kept elsewhere is replaced; (5) a ticked set retyped far off: after Fix it the set stayed unticked once its load was fixed, and Log it started its rest over (5 s left became 150 s): the set unticked for the question is ticked again by Log it or by the fixed load, its rest left running (`setDone`'s `again`, `ui.refix`); (6) a unit glued to the x ("100kgx8") or pounds written as # ("225#") was not read as the line's own unit (r54 the same): read now. Found and not built in r55, each older than r55: (a) two workouts in progress at a first sign-in: when the account's phone ticks a set before it next pulls, its newer workout replaces the new phone's in the account and the new phone's ticked sets are lost, the new phone being told both that the other's workout was saved and that its own was replaced (r54 loses them at the sign-in itself): r56, with the two-phones rework; (b) a phone signed out of one account keeping a copy, then signed in to another, merges the first account's log into the second, and a deletion made in the second reaches the first at its next sign-in, held there in Recently deleted (r54 the same once the app is reopened): for V's decision, proposed for r55.1: a phone holding another account's log asks whether to add it to this account or start empty; (c) a set ticked while signed out on a workout another phone also ticked is lost: r56; (d) demo data loaded while signed in is uploaded to the account (r54 the same): r55.1. Not checked by the review: WebKit; Undo inside a session after the untick of (5), and with the effort strip open; supersets, past-session edits, lighter and deload days on the session screen; two tabs on one phone after Sign out with Keep; a sign-out from an expired or revoked token (it keeps the notes, by design: signing back in to that account merges from them); Delete account with a workout in progress; CSV and .xlsx imports (pasted text only); real network latency.

#### r54, live since 2026-10-20 (pull request #47, merged by V; build 2026.10.18-r54)
Version 2.5: item 315's fixes under the way of working in 316, reviewed independently (318), every finding fixed with a test.

309. V's look at the r52 preview (2026-10-18), three reports, each run with the real buttons on r52's and r51's previews against a stand-in account with a 31-session log. (a) and (b): skipped the account, went back and signed in, or signed in straight away, and the preview showed nothing. Found: the preview syncs to its own table (r24, V's choice of 2026-09-30), so a sign-in brings the real log only through Copy my log in; r51's preview does the same, and the live app on a new phone opens on the log. A fix in `scripts/cloud-supabase.js` (copy the live rows into the preview table at a preview sign-in when the preview holds no session) was built, then taken out the same day after the independent review: it overwrote what the preview held besides sessions (weigh-ins, cardio, routines made there), it read the phone's preview log by a narrower test than the app's own first-link rule, so a weigh-in from Set up was lost, and it could hold the sign-in sheet busy with no time limit. The copy belongs in the app, through Copy my log in's own path (it asks before replacing, and has Undo), when a preview sign-in brings no log; that path is legacy code (the account actions and `cloudInit`), so it is built when the account flow moves (307) or in a release that moves that code first (ARCHITECTURE.md, A change to the legacy script between phases), with tests for each case the review found. Meanwhile: after signing in to the preview, Copy my log in (on the Set up page and in Settings > Your data). (c): a wrong email or password left the sign-in sheet "kind of stuck": not reproduced in Chromium on r51 or r52 (the sheet says Wrong email or password, retyping signs in, the cross closes it); WebKit cannot run on Claude's machine. V (2026-10-18): (a) and (b) are as expected, the preview keeping its own log; (c) was the email field: after Wrong email or password it could not be tapped to change it, so the sheet had to be closed and opened again, and sheets sit behind the keyboard generally. Both were set for phase 5 with the account sheet; V's 317 (a) brought them into r54, built there (311 f): sheets sit in the part of the screen that shows, above the keyboard, and the sign-in sheet keeps its own fields through Wrong email or password. A sign-in request that never answers would still leave the sheet on Signing in, with no time limit (the same gap as 306's report sheet; 307).
311. Built in r54 (V's 317 (a): all of it), each case in tests/r54b.js failing on r53 (`node tests/r54b.js baselines/r53.html`) unless it says it guards: (a) a PR on a lighter day or in a deload is marked and announced as the set is logged, as on any day (`refreshPR`), and a bodyweight lift before any weigh-in has its rep PRs listed and announced, as the Guide says (`idxPRsIn`'s `big`, `livePR`); (b) Short on time marks nothing: under the one rule (312 E) a lift with fewer sets than usual is read so from the log (`idxShort`), repeats its target and counts for its trend; (c) a blank session in a week marked as a deload is a deload, an exercise added to it at half its sets (`startFree`); (d) nothing is stored as trimmed, so a session saved as trimmed whose forgotten sets were added in History is read from its sets, a full session that sets the next target; (e) Goal reads every set of the last 12 weeks, a lighter day's and a deload's too, as the bests do (`goalState`); (f) the sign-in sheet redrawn while signing in keeps its own fields (`morphInto`, `src/app/sheets.ts`), so the field in use is never replaced under the keyboard and can be tapped after Wrong email or password (309 c); only the account sheet keeps its fields on a redraw, every other sheet still rebuilt from its markup, each to take the same once it is checked. Sheets sitting above the keyboard was built here too and taken out after V's look at the preview (322); (g) Finish keeps an injury's hold on the saved exercise (`inj`, kept by `normalize` with `KNOWN_SEX`, absent unless held, a migration case from r53), and the stall test leaves held sessions out, so a lift held for an injury since cleared does not read stalled.
312. V's calls from the same audit, built in r54 as V answered (317), each case in tests/r54b.js: (E) one simple rule for every session (V 2026-10-18): a lift sets its next target only at its usual load and reps for its usual sets; fewer sets (`short: 'sets'`) or a clearly weaker reading (`short: 'reading'`, a bad day, 317 (e)) repeat the target and still count for its trend, stalls and PRs, two bad days in a row and the app follows; done lighter (a deload, a Lighter day or read lighter from its loads) it sets no target or trend; a lift's usual is the median of its two sessions before and this one in its range since its last break, its usual sets those of most of the last three, never more than the plan (317 f; `idxShort`, `lighter`, `offTarget`, `sessionWord`), replacing the trimmed mark and r30's half rule; a break of more than four weeks restarts a lift's readings (`afterBreak`: the lighter reading, its usual, the trend and the stall), and the first session back sets the next target (until r54 it read lighter against the sessions before the break, so the next target came from them: August's 100 kg after 90 kg back, in tests/r54b.js), the lift reading as back until a session after the break sets one (`backDaysIn`); (A) a lift trained in two rep ranges reads stalled only when it is stalled in every range in use (`stallScopesIn`: the newest range and the routine's planned ranges with a session in the last 42 days), for the span of the shorter stretch; (B) settled by (E): a past workout with fewer sets than usual repeats its target; (C) a swap keeps the slot's plan, as before (317 c); (D) a lift done in full on a lighter day counts in full, as r53 built and V kept. Still open, for the record: (F) in a week read as lighter, any load under the previous three's maximum reads lighter, with no threshold (r30's second pass), and a week read lighter leaves all its sets out of the averages; the time model reads rest and links from today's routine, not the session's.
313. Built in r54 (317 (a)), each case in tests/r54b.js: (a) a lift on no day of the routine takes its plan from its last session that sets a target, as the analytics read it (an untagged deload read lighter from its loads is passed over), since `planForIn` moved to layer 2 (`src/app/plans.ts`; `basePlanIn` stays in `routines.ts`); (a2) a lift done in full on a lighter day is such a session; (b) a lift counted in full on a lighter day is one of the sessions a later one is read lighter against; (c) the usual-load test reads the working load, so a heavy single left out on a lighter day is no reason to read it lighter; (d) that plan is read up to the date logged, so an exercise added to a past workout reads its sessions up to that date, and a session with fewer sets than usual sets nothing; (e) a week tagged as a deload whose every lift was done in full is no lighter week and stays in the weekly averages; (g) Finish's offer after a lighter day or deload adds an exercise at the count Pick for me chose, kept on the block (`full`), as after a normal day; (h) Finish's question about sets left says the one rule. (f) stays as recorded: `tests/r53w.js` is pinned to r53 since r54 and is not changed again, and `tests/r53.js` holds the coach's next branch by name.
314. Built in r54, tests/r39.js: on r53 a sheet closed while the body was still turning let the turn go on, on Today's body, so a check that closed the sheet after a fixed 700 ms, under a full run where frames come late, could find Today's body turned: the likeliest cause of the check that failed now and then (shown on r53 by closing just before a turn ended). Closing the sheet now stops the turn (`closeModal`), each turn is waited for until it ends, not for a time, and a new check closes the sheet mid-turn, which fails on r53 (`node tests/r39.js baselines/r53.html`; the suite read no page argument before r54).
315. The whole-app audit (V, 2026-10-18: matching the release before is not the same as being right): a simulated lifter took four journeys through the real page, a beginner on the starter routine, an intermediate over 12 weeks with every flow, kg and lb with RPE, an assisted lift, a plank, a layoff and an injury, and r53's own rules, with what a lifter expects written down first. 11 problems, every one present in r52 already, none made worse by r53; held for r54 (V: ship the reviewed r53 first), and built there, tests/r54.js, each case failing on r53 (`node tests/r54.js baselines/r53.html`), all but (i) and (k): (a) dumbbells in kg with Your gym not set: a first session's adjustment after 6 kg done easily is 8.5 kg, which no rack has, and the 6, 8.5 and 11 kg logged after read as a rack in 0.5 kg steps for twelve sessions; (b) a log kept in kg and read in lb: one converted load in five stays off the plate grid ("add 5 lb" to 93.7 lb), dumbbells round up 9% under a target of reps, and the step in the words does not match the load shown; (c) on the RPE scale the reason behind a target says RIR; (d) "Stalled for 3 weeks" is fixed text, on the coach (built) and on the Stats headline (`exHeadline`, legacy); (e) following the plan exactly still shows muscles below target and "add sets": V chose to fix all three causes, one reading of a lighter week for the averages, the Stats line and the coach (built for the averages and the coach; the Stats line's own reading by hard sets and its tip are in `viewDash`), the first week left out of the averages while loads are found (the radar's label, `regionSrc`, legacy), and "add sets" only when the plan itself is short (built); (f) an every-other-day routine (3.5 sessions a week) needs 4 every week, so its streak never builds: V chose 3 as on plan for the ring, the streak and adherence alike (adherence is in `viewDash`); (g) Build my own routine makes a 3-day cycle with no rest days, so the app plans 7 sessions a week (`ACT.obOwn`); (h) a past workout logged for a day before Deload week was tapped opens and saves as a deload (`pastDraft`; the tap date is not stored); (i) an assisted lift's first session ticked with no help typed logs it at full bodyweight, and the next normal session then reads lighter; (j) History shows "(deload) deload"; (k) after a partial bad day (10, 9, 8 then 7, 6, 6, 5) the next target is to beat the bad day, which r54's one rule (312) settles by repeating the target. Also seen: a 155 lb set after 70 kg logs a Weight PR made of unit rounding. What r54 does: (a) with nothing logged, the load in hand is the rack's one known weight, so 6 kg steps to 8 kg; (b) a load counts as typed only when exact in the unit on screen, a target of reps rounds down, and the words name the step the shown load took; (c) the engine's reasons say effort in the lifter's scale (effScale, effAt); (d) the coach and the Stats headline say the stall test's own stretch (stallStretch, stallWeeksIn), and the headline's tip gives the rule (STALL_WHY); (e) one reading of a lighter week (lighterTally, idxLightWeeks) for the averages, the deload suggestion and Stats' last lighter week, which also counts a week with nothing trained, as Stats did; the log's first week left out of the averages (avgFirstWeekIn), said by the coach, the radar, Volume and the Guide (FIRST_WEEK_WHY); and the coach says to do every planned set when the plan already reaches the target; (f) a cycle plans the whole number of sessions under its rate (plannedPerWeek), at least one; (g) Build my own routine makes Training, Rest, Training, Rest, Training, Rest, Rest; (h) a past workout is never set up as a deload; (j) History names a deload once; (i), built later in r54 (tests/r54b.js): an assisted lift with nothing logged shows no help in grey and its empty field no 0 (`sgFirstIn`, `emptyLoad`), and Finish asks for the help used before saving a set without it; (k) settled by the one rule (312 E): a bad day repeats the target; and the 155 lb rounding PR, built (tests/r54b.js): a set is judged for its PRs at the earlier load typed in the other unit that it stands for, when it lies above it by no more than half the spacing the set's own load shows (`prSameLoad`, `typedUnit`, `LOAD_STEPS`; a convention, in the code and the Guide), in the PR list, as the set is logged and in the toast's numbers alike (`prFrameIn`), only ever lighter, so a change of units or gyms is never a PR and a step past the old load, or a rep more at the same weight, still is; the toast and the list show the estimate as logged. Noted while building (f), put to V: This week's planned hard sets for a cycle were the cycle's weekly average (Strength 5 x 5: 46, three and a half sessions' worth), so a week of 3 sessions, on plan by the ring, read 37 or 41 of 46; V chose the 3 sessions (39), built (317 d).
316. How work is done from r54 (V, 2026-10-18, on a second opinion V brought and the plan put to V): keep strict types, the module layers, the behaviour suites, the check that screens agree, the WebKit runs, and the highest bar for storage and sync, since a lost log cannot be rebuilt; one independent review a release, for correctness and data loss; old code fixed in place with a failing test first, and a screen moved to the modules only when it is being changed anyway; the token-for-token move checks and the tests of the tests dropped; CLAUDE.md cut to the rules in force, its history moved to an archive read when needed, and this list cut to what is open. The comparisons with r35 and r52 stay, pinned to the builds they proved. r53 shipped under the old rules, as reviewed. Built in r54: CLAUDE.md rewritten (its history and code map in docs/ARCHIVE.md, the evidence in docs/EVIDENCE.md), tests/moved.js deleted, tests/r53w.js pinned to baselines/r53.html, and this list's Done section moved to docs/ARCHIVE.md.
317. V's answers of 2026-10-18 (evening), for r54, built with tests: (a) everything open goes in r54: the one rule (312 E), all of 311, 313, 315 (i) and (k), the 155 lb rounding PR, 314; (b) a lift trained in two rep ranges reads stalled only when every range in use is (312 A); (c) a swap keeps the slot's plan, as now (312 C: a swap fills that day's slot, so it takes its sets, range, reps in reserve and rest; the load still comes from the lift's own history); (d) on a cycle, This week's planned hard sets and the plan's sets a week follow the whole sessions the week plans (Strength 5 x 5: 39, not 46), which also settles the review's finding below; (e) the one rule counts a bad day as falling short: one session clearly under its usual repeats the target, two in a row and the app follows; (f) a lift's usual sets are what it did in most of its last three sessions, never more than the plan; (g) with Your gym not set, lb dumbbells are read on a 5 lb rack (12 lb steps to 15, 8 to 10, 2.5 lb steps when the weights are on them). The independent review of r54's first part (commit a8cb6c3) found, each reproduced: (1) a reps target in a log kept in the other unit rounded down with no floor (2 kg dumbbells to 0 lb, the 20 kg bar to 40 lb, 4 kg to 5 lb, a session the next one read as lighter, so its reps never counted); (2) on a cycle that is not a week long, the coach said do every planned set to a lifter doing them, the planned sets being three and a half sessions' worth while three counted as a week on plan; (3) after a first week begun on a Monday, Stats said Finish a full week beside the coach's after your second week, Recent average said No sessions in the previous 4 full weeks, and in week 3 Averaged over the 1 week with training in the last 4; (4) a cycle of one training day in 8 to 14 planned none a week (fixed before the review ended); (5) lb dumbbells with nothing logged stepped 1 lb (r53 did no better). No data-loss or sync problem. What r54 does for them, each with tests failing on r53: (1) a load off the step in the unit on screen goes to the nearest step, never under the bar or one step on a loaded lift, nor a tenth or more under the load done but on lighter days (`snapUnitIn`); (2) after (d), a cycle's weeks still differ in their days (Mon, Wed and Fri on Strength 5 x 5 hold two A days one week and one the next), so an average of 1 or 3 weeks read five muscles below target: a muscle under its minimum only by which days fell in the weeks averaged, every session and set due done and the plan reaching the target, is on plan (`cycleMixIn`), read one way by the coach, Stats' summary, Muscles, Volume, the radar, the map and a muscle's headline (`muscleStatusIn`; before, each judged it itself); and a first-run pick of Strength 5 x 5 before r54 fitted chest, triceps and upper back to 8 by the old reading, now above the plan's 7.5, so at start the app's own targets follow r54's fit and its floors are recorded, typed targets left alone (`refitIn`, tests/migration.js from r53 and r43); (3) one reading of why no average shows yet, said alike by the coach, Stats' summary, Recent average and a muscle's headline (`avgWaitIn`), and the weeks averaged named after the first week wherever they are said (`avgWeeksTxt`); (5) V's (g), built: lb dumbbells read on a 5 lb rack, 2.5 lb where the weights are on it, a weight off it stepping to the rack's next one in every path (`rackGridIn`), and the lightest dumbbells kept on a Lighter day or deload, where r53 gave 0 lb or 0 kg.
318. The independent review of r54 (commit 2478d73 against r53's 41aa4a1, in Chromium) found three problems, each new in r54 and reproduced beside r53, and no data-loss, sync, crash or layout problem: (1) every planned set done, but the session read as fewer sets when the load changed within it, as its sets were counted only at the working load: 100 x 10, 9 and 9 with 97.5 x 9; 100 x 9, 9 then 102.5 x 7, whose beaten target was held back; and the starter's bench with set 1 stopped at 3 and the rest at the 82.5 the app itself lowered it to; (2) a set past the plan or the lifter's usual count held back a target the planned sets beat, as the session's reading averaged every set at the working load while the engine's target reads the planned sets (50 x 11, 10 and 10 with a set of 5: 31+ again, not 32+); (3) the stall's weeks on Stats and in the coach counted sessions held for an injury, which the stall test leaves out ("over the last 4 weeks" of a stretch that ended before the injury). Found while fixing them: (4) the same extra set read a Lighter day done at the usual load as lighter (r53 did too). What r54 does, each case in tests/r54b.js failing on the build reviewed but a guard: a session's sets are its working sets within a tenth of its working load either way and any heavier one at its usual load (`setsAt`), so the sessions in (1) are full or below usual, and a back-off set a tenth lighter is still no usual set (the guard); each of those bounds has a case that fails without it; a session is compared with its usual over as many of its sets at the working load as the usual sets (`readingOf`, `repsOf`), in the one rule and in the usual-load test of a lighter day; the stall's span and its words come from the sessions the test reads (`stallReads`, `stallWordsIn`), "over 5 weeks" when held sessions came after the stretch. The Guide and docs/EVIDENCE.md say how sets count. Checked by the review and found right: normalize between r53 and r54 (no rewrite loop), History edits and Short on time's Undo keeping the new fields, the account sheet while signing in, six sheets at two sizes in both themes, the start-up refit leaving default targets alone, the demo log, the touched flows with no page errors, and PRs after a change of units. Not checked by it: WebKit, a real on-screen keyboard, a two-device sync, imports and the Supabase functions.
320. Found after the review, while fixing it (r54): the engine suite's coverage of the coach fell by a line the fixes merged, and the lines it had never run were two of the coach's (every muscle at target with nothing stalled, and a muscle's strength falling with nothing stalled) and `musTrend`; tests/engine.js now runs histories made for those lines and calls `musTrend` in Node against the page. Building them showed a wrong line, fixed with a case in tests/r54b.js that fails on r53 and on the build reviewed: on a lift trained on two days at two rep ranges, the coach and Stats read "at the top of its range" against the plan's range, the first day's, so the light day's 60 x 12 x 3, the bottom of 12 to 15, read as the top of the heavy day's 6 to 10 and "due a step up"; both now read the plan of the day the last session was done on, found by its day or by the range it was done at, through one test (`atRangeTopIn`); a lift on one day reads as before. With that fixed, no wide history reached the coach's top-of-range line (each that had, had compared a session's sets with another day's range), so a third history reaches it, and every line of `coach.ts` runs (231 of 231). It also showed the trend flipping with the day done last, V's call (319). And two races in tests/journey.js with the auto-reorder (r45), which moves an exercise started below ones not yet touched 0.9 s after its first tick: on a busy machine the move landed between the reload check's two looks, the orders differing and nothing lost (once in a run of eleven suites beside the engine suite, once in four runs side by side), or mid-step, so the steps after it found another exercise in its place (5 of 12 runs four at a time beside three busy loops). The suite now waits for the move and finds the exercise again: 12 of 12 under the same load. And on GitHub, pull request #47's first run: every Chromium shard failed before a suite ran, as the workflow still ran tests/moved.js, retired in r54 (316), while every suite passed here, where nothing runs the workflow; WebKit passed in all four shards. The workflow runs hygiene alone now, and tests/hygiene.js checks that every script a workflow runs exists, a check that fails on that workflow.
321. V on the r54 preview (2026-10-19, iPhone, Safari): with the keyboard up, the sign-in and Create account sheets showed the page's own button and text under them, "showing twice". Caused by r54's own fix for the keyboard (311 f): the sheets' layer was sized to the visual viewport, and iOS 26 Safari leaves out of it the strip behind its glass address pill and the bar above the keyboard while still drawing the page there. Now the layer covers the whole screen as before r54, its sheet sits at the bottom of the part that shows, and the strip below is the sheet's own colour (`--vv-under`, measured by a fixed element, `src/app/sheets.ts`; `#modal::after`); tests/r54b.js checks every point of that strip is the sheet's layer, in its colour, which fails on the build V saw (a Chromium screenshot with a stand-in keyboard showed the same as V's). r54's checks had asked only that the sheet sat in the part that shows, never what showed below it. Found the same day: the first rest's one-time tip about a locked screen took the place of a PR's message 0.6 s after the set (`wakeTip`); it waits now for a message showing to go, with a case in tests/r54b.js that fails before it (a check there had failed on it once in four runs). That strip was taken out with the rest of the keyboard work the same day (322).
322. V on the r54 preview again (2026-10-19, after 321): signing in, the page moved up and down slowly as the keyboard came, and Forgot password and the links at the bottom of the sheet were hidden; then, on the build that put sheets back as r53 placed them: "prior version doesnt mean its correct", "fix it for all devices homescreen app installs and browsers". No browser here has an on-screen keyboard, so r54's two changes to how sheets meet it (sized by the visual viewport, then that with the strip of 321) were checked only with a stand-in, and on the iPhone both were worse. Found by research the same day: Safari on an iPhone does not shrink the page for the keyboard (`interactive-widget=resizes-content` is in WebKit's source but not shipped in Safari, bram.us, 2026-09-11), and iOS 26 has open bugs that move fixed elements around the keyboard (WebKit bug 297779; Apple's developer forums, thread 800154, `visualViewport.offsetTop` left above 0 after the keyboard closes), so code that follows the keyboard fights iOS. Built: no code follows the keyboard (`followViewport` and its styles taken out), and a sheet you type in opens tall, from just under the status bar to the bottom of the screen, its fields and its buttons at the top, read in the style sheet from the sheet's own fields (`:has`), on phones and touch screens; a field near the top is never under the keyboard, so iOS scrolls nothing and nothing moves, and the sheet's own surface fills the screen below, so no part of the page shows. Sheets with nothing to type in, the picker (its search already at its top) and a sheet on a desktop are as before. Kept: the sign-in sheet keeping its fields through Wrong email or password (311 f) and the first rest's tip waiting for a PR's message (321). tests/r54b.js: the sign-in, create account and problem report sheets open tall at 390 x 844 and 320 x 568, the first line of every field in the top 240 px, which fails on the build V saw; the menu, the picker and a desktop sheet as before (guards); nothing moves with a stand-in keyboard. Left: on the iPhone in Safari, the space above the keyboard and Safari's bars is about 280 points, so with the keyboard up the Sign in button sits at its edge (Go on the keyboard signs in) and the links under it show when the keyboard closes; in the home-screen app there is more room. V checks it on the phone (OWNER.md). Found by the full run: a sheet scrolled to its very bottom lost 4 px when redrawn (r53 too, 226 to 222), as the labels' i buttons, which change its height, were added after the scroll was put back; they are added first now (`renderModal`), tests/flows.js redrawing at the very bottom.

#### r53, live since 2026-10-19 (pull request #46, merged by V; build 2026.10.18-r53)
Version 2.4, a behaviour release between phases 3b and 4, reviewed six times independently.

310. Built in r53 (V 2026-10-18: "tied at exercise level", "all, not most", and "if you hit a pr that is a pr no matter when"), tests/r53.js, each rule failing on r52 first: a lift done at its usual load and reps, for as many sets, on a lighter day or in a deload is a full session for that lift (its target, trend, stalls and Last line), read against its last full sessions in the range, up to three, by its strength reading where it has one and by its reps a set where it has none (a hold, a leg raise, pull-ups before any weigh-in); a PR counts on any day, shown at Finish; a lift on no day of the routine starts from its own last full session, its sets and, where Your training sets none, the range it was done at; Finish's offer after a lighter day or a deload adds the full sets; a PR on a session left out of a lift's stall (trimmed or done lighter) since the third-last session of its stretch counts as movement, as one in the stretch does; and from the audit V asked for the same day, no stall while an injury the lift loads is flagged, and the coach holding back a deload after a lighter week read from what the lifts did (more than half its sessions with half their lifts done lighter, once it ends or from the second, or, a deload begun mid-week, its last two or more sessions deloads done lighter), a rest week, or a marked deload week with nothing trained in it, no longer after one Lighter day or one deload session in a week otherwise trained. The Guide says each. Different from the plan written here before: the reps in reserve stay the plan's or the profile's (last time's as the target would hide a load that was too light, which the engine reads from sets 2 or more over the target), and the range is the one the session was planned at, not one guessed from its reps as an import does (`routineFromLog`), so a session with no range on record (an import) starts from the default range. Found before release, each fixed and tested: by r53's own checks, the first version read a lift with no strength reading at its usual load whatever its reps, so 8 leg raises on a lighter day after 12s counted in full and the next target fell from 25+ reps to 17+; by the independent review, half the sets at the usual load counted in full, so two sets of 10 after four sets of 10, 10, 9 and 8 made the next target add load off half the work, and a deload begun mid-week (a normal Monday, deloads on Wednesday and Friday) left the coach asking for another deload the next week, since the week was not all tagged; by the second review, half the sets still counted in full when one earlier session had stopped at half, a deload week marked on a Saturday after a normal week held the coach back for four weeks with no deload done, deload sessions done at full for every lift did too, and two Lighter days early in a week did not; by the third, one deload session late in a normal week held the coach back where one Lighter day did not; by the fourth, a four-day lifter who tapped Deload week on Wednesday and deloaded Thursday and Friday was asked for a deload again (a week whose last two or more sessions were lighter now counts); by the fifth, that rule hung on the weekday, two Lighter days on Thursday and Friday after three normal days holding the deload back where the same on Monday and Tuesday did not, and a heavy and light programme with its light days late in the week never getting one (only deloads make that run now), the target note and the stall tip said nothing of fewer reps on a lighter day or of Make it lighter, and a lift read stalled the day after a PR on a Lighter day, beside it in Recent PRs (r52 did the same after a trimmed session); by the sixth, that fix ending a stall on a PR no screen shows (90 x 9 on a Lighter day after 100 x 8), which took a needed deload away again (now only a PR Recent PRs lists, on a lift with a reading), the deload run missing a Freestyle lifter's deload begun mid-week (now any session in the marked week counts as a deload session), and the stall tip and Guide promising no PR on any day where the stall reads one rep range (now said for the range). Moved code changed after its move through `edits` in tests/moved.js (ARCHITECTURE.md, A change to the legacy script between phases).

#### r48, live since 2026-10-16 (pull request #41, merged by V; build 2026.10.16-r48)
301. Built in r48 (V 2026-10-16, "a perfecting sweep, no breakage"): the research report of 2026-10-16 was checked against the code and against the papers themselves on PubMed, and the app already met every benchmark that had a source. What changed is the record, not a number: the 2026 ACSM position stand is cited from its full text (its twice-a-week finding is about strength, not growth, so no routine is called better than another for growth); the per-session comment credited the weekly paper ("Pelland et al. (2024)", its preprint year) for the per-session finding (Remmert 2025, preprint) and said "above 10" where the code flags above 11, both fixed; a comment saying estimates run low on deadlifts and high on leg presses had no source and is gone; the helper tip and the Guide now say that which muscles count as helpers on a lift is the library's own call (Pelland 2025 measured direct against indirect sets and says nothing about grip or holding muscles); Singer 2024 and Ramos-Campo 2024 are in the evidence base with their numbers. The report's rules with no source behind them (at most 3 helpers, 2 long-length exercises a muscle, a 6 to 10 set beginner band) were not adopted. Every ready-made routine was counted by the app's own rule: the starter trains each major muscle twice a week inside 10 to 20 sets, and no routine puts more than 11 sets on one muscle in a session. r47 is saved as a baseline. No suite changed, since no behaviour did.

#### r47, live since 2026-10-15 (pull request #40, merged by V; build 2026.10.15-r47), tests/r47.js, tests/bests.js, tests/perf.js
299. Built in r47, tests/coherence.js, tests/r47.js (V 2026-10-15, "pipelines, flows, calculations and consistency must align"): the sessions on each date are one index (`dateIndex`, `sessionsOn`) read by the week bar, the calendar, the day picker, This month's title and Consistency's heatmap, in one order; until now each grouped the log itself, and the week bar named a two-a-day's evening session first (by id) where the day picker named the morning first (by start time). Tapping such a day on the week bar now asks which session, as the calendar always did, instead of opening one. This week's hard sets read IDX's `weekHard`, the table the recap, Volume and the muscle views count from, instead of a second copy of the rule. A crash now reaches the owner in one tap: Report this on the recovery screen opens the problem report written, with the error and the stack, and the report survives a log that cannot be read. What the log keeps never holds a URL's query or fragment (a reset link carries its token there for a moment), and a report trims its oldest errors to stay under the feedback table's 20,000-byte limit, which would otherwise refuse it. What is not alerting: the owner still learns of a crash when the lifter taps the button; nobody is paged. That needs a server, and is deferred on purpose (item 290's third risk).
298. Built in r47, tests/bests.js (V 2026-10-15): a lift's heaviest set shows while you train it, under the Last line, in the bests board's words, with the date only when the Last line does not already carry it. It was two taps away, under the exercise's menu. The Guide now says a target is not a record: a target is what to beat today, a record is the best ever done, a PR is the moment one is set. With nothing logged, Today points at History's Log a past workout, which is how lifts done before the app come in: they are ordinary sessions, so they set bests, show under Stats, and the engine starts from them. Checked end to end: 225x5 logged for last year gives heaviest 225 with an estimate above it, and the engine reads it as a layoff to start from.
300. Built in r47, tests/r47.js (V 2026-10-15, after asking lifters at the gym what they wanted most): every target says why it is that number. `whyTarget(b)` is the engine's own sentence (`b.hint` less the Last line the screen already shows, plus the basis note), behind an i named Why this target, on the session screen and on Stats' Next session. Before this the engine's sentence showed only when there was no target at all, so on r46 none of ten targets on a demo session was explained. A check in tests/coherence.js keeps the shown reason word for word the engine's, so the two cannot drift.
297. Built in r47, tests/perf.js: how long the app takes at three years of training, against ceilings about four times what was measured, so a change several times worse fails. Measured: ticking a set 21 ms, Stats 70 ms, an analytics rebuild 91 ms (desktop; a phone is roughly 3 to 4 times slower). Ticking a set, the one a lifter waits on, was the worry and is fine; it grows roughly with the log (3 ms at 3 months, 7 ms at a year, 21 ms at 3 years).

245. Settled (four points the r34.1 review left for V): (a) the second change number went in r35 (248) and r41 (266); (b) an easy set with no heavier load that fits now gets more reps at the same load (r35, 252); (c) at 320 px seven 44 px days cannot fit 288 px, so the set row, week bar and calendar days are 37 to 40 px there and 44 px from 375 px (kept); (d) a superset made in a session is offered to the plan at Finish (r35, 253).
41. Setting: count RIR 4-5 as half a set (off by default). Built in r40, item 261.

#### r46, live since 2026-10-14 (pull request #37, merged by V; build 2026.10.14-r46), tests/r46.js, tests/coherence.js and WebKit in the checks
296. Added to r46's pull request (2026-10-14): every suite also runs in WebKit, the engine of every iPhone browser, on GitHub (`npm run test:webkit`, four shards; the `test` check needs them, so nothing WebKit fails is published). Until now only Chromium ran. Getting it to pass needed harness changes only, no app change: a file page served its own files, object URLs let through, finger drags on SortableJS's Safari path (`touchDriver`), a blank-space tap kept clear of tappable things, and the worker's offline load reports left out while offline. Not covered there: the home-screen mode, the on-screen keyboard and Safari's storage eviction; those stay on the phone (item 7). The Claude artifact's mentions in CLAUDE.md, README.md and PROMPT.md now say it was retired on 2026-09-27.
295. Built in r46, tests/r46.js (the r45 quality check, V 2026-10-14): one 4-week window for sessions and PRs on Today, the Stats tiles and Recent PRs (they read 30, 28 and 14 days); the 4 weeks before only once the log covers them; adherence counts a two-a-day as two, as the streak does; a rest week shows no ring; Finish syncs the session before the emptied draft, so another phone never says it was discarded; an edit replaced from another phone says so; the wait message no longer names a code on Create account and Reset password; two copy fixes; dates and week counts computed once (Stats and Today render faster, same numbers). Root causes rather than patches: `draftNote` states only what it can see instead of guessing discarded (a finished workout whose session document failed to upload read as discarded), and every exercise in a session carries an id of its own, so the confirm window, the pending auto-mark and the fold timer survive both a move and a rebuild (holding the exercise object instead was tried first and was no better: `normalize` replaces every block on each sync and Undo). A rest week is reported once by `weekStats` instead of each reader testing for it, which left the planned target on the card under the words Rest week. `tests/coherence.js` now checks the screens against each other on every build, since every finding here was of that shape and every suite was green: it fails 8 checks on r38 through r44 and 7 on r45.

#### r44, live since 2026-10-13 (pull request #35, merged by V; build 2026.10.13-r44), tests/r44.js, tests/security.js and tests/SUITES.md
271. Built in r44, tests/r44.js: Your training, an optional profile (goal: Size, Strength or Both; years lifting; days a week; minutes per session, which is the time budget moved into it; up to two focus muscles). V's three answers were not given by the time of the build, so it follows Claude's recommendations, each still V's to change before merging: (1) it sets defaults and new plan items only, and the routine in use changes only when V taps Apply (it shows how many exercises differ, asks first, has Undo); (2) weekly targets V typed stay as typed, and only the app's own move (exactly: the app records the floors a first-run routine pick set, so it never guesses which targets were typed; a Default targets button resets them, with Undo; targets fitted before r44 count as typed); (3) asked in Settings (Your training, the first group) and, on a first run, as a folded, optional line at the top of the routine picker that Set up opens (goal, years, days; the days mark the routines that fit right there). It is not on the Set up page itself: that page's text is held under 400 characters (r21), and the line would have pushed it over. What it sets, each with its source in the Guide and CLAUDE.md: Strength puts barbell and machine compounds at 3 to 6 reps, 2 in reserve, 3 minutes' rest, Both at 5 to 8; under a year, 8 to 12 for every exercise with 3 or 2 in reserve; Size keeps every default exactly as before. Focus muscles (item 43) 30% up, the other major muscles at a third of the usual minimum, back to the defaults when the focus comes off. Strength focus in Pick for me (item 44): a compound stalled or within 3% of its best, rested and not done for 5 days, ranks higher. Days a week marks the ready-made routines that fit. With no profile, every number is r43's.
43. Specialisation: Focus on up to 2 muscles. Built in r44 as part of 271.
44. Pick for me "Strength focus". Built in r44 as part of 271.
281. Built in r44, pull request #35, alongside the profile (V, 2026-10-13: "more efficient across the board"; one release, not two), no change to the app: suites run side by side (`tests/run.js`: both builds once up front, suites on this machine's cores, time-sensitive ones one at a time after, per-suite seconds, `--jobs` and `--shard`), and on GitHub in four shards on four runners at once, with one `test` check that passes only when all four did; `tests/h.js` `build()` so no suite rebuilds dist/ under another. CLAUDE.md: an Invariants section first (every never, always and V ruling from the code map), the suite table moved to `tests/SUITES.md` (a third shorter; tests/hygiene.js keeps a row for every suite). Not done, on purpose: optimizing app code. Measured on two years of history (546 sessions, 12,000 sets): the slowest moment is opening Stats after a change, about 0.2 s on a desktop; ticking a set redraws nothing; there are no dead or duplicate functions. Revisit if a phone feels slow.
282. Built in r44 (V, 2026-10-13: "what I did last time next to what I'm about to do"), tests/r44.js: a Last column in the session, each row beside set n of last time at its working load (the sets the grey reps repeat); the line above the sets names the session only, unless a top single or a pyramid's other sets were left out of the column. The set number marks a warm-up or a drop (tap once, twice), so the W/D column is gone and the load and reps fields keep r43's width within 1 px at 320 px. Nothing new is saved with a session.
283. Built in r44 (V, 2026-10-13: "make sure the code does not have security flaws, prove it"), tests/security.js on both builds: every typed text field as markup through every tab, sheet and search; checked in the same pass: no secrets in the 121 tracked files, the 192 commits of history or the saved builds; the one key in the repo is Supabase's publishable key, public by design, with the build refusing a secret one. The feedback sheet now writes its screenshot only when it is the app's own JPEG. Left as is, said plainly: the old Claude artifact loads Chart.js and Sortable from public CDNs without an integrity check (the installed app loads nothing from other sites); the hashes could not be fetched from the build machine, and the artifact is no longer in use. The preview shares the live app's origin, so its code could reach the live app's storage on the same phone; a separate preview domain would wall it off (V, 2026-10-13: not now).

#### r43, live since 2026-10-13 (pull request #34, merged by V; build 2026.10.13-r43), tests/r43.js
278. Built in r43: a phone keeps the fields it does not know when it loads or syncs the log, instead of dropping them (`normalize` rebuilt every record from the fields it knew, so an r37 phone wiped a lift goal set on r38). Kept on every kind of record, only as plain data, under safe names, at most 20 fields and 2,000 characters per record, in the order they came (so two builds never rewrite each other's copy), never shown. A save with nothing unknown comes out exactly as on r42, so nothing re-syncs. Editing a session from History keeps the session's and each exercise's fields; the sets are what the edit rewrites. An independent review before release found a sync rewrite loop with a future build, a size cap that counted values only, supersets read 30% fast and in-session cardio counted as lifting; each fixed with a test that fails without the fix. Only helps from r43 on: phones still on r42 or older drop what they do not know, so every device updates before a new field is relied on.
279. Built in r43: estimated minutes (Today, Plan, the session's minutes left, Short on time, Pick for me, the recap) follow how long your own sessions take against the time model, once 3 sessions timed in the app in the last 12 weeks exist: the typical (median) one of the newest 8, leaving out any at under a third or over three times the model (a clock left on or stopped); supersets rest once, as in the estimates, and cardio done before or after lifting comes off the session's time. Before that, the model as before. Stats' Average session tile drops "planned ~N min" while your pace is in use (it would read as your average again). Said in the Guide's Today topic.

#### r34.2, live since 2026-10-11 (pull request #24, merged by V); tests/r34b.js
238. Done (item 230): three independent reviewers worked through r34.1 on 2026-10-11 as a new user (first page, layout at 320 to 430 px, wording), a plan follower in lb (9 weeks, supersets, drops, deload, rest week, RPE), and a freestyler in kg plus a calisthenics lifter (6 to 8 weeks, two-a-days, a dumbbell rack). 55 findings (1 critical, 4 high). Each is fixed at its cause in r34.2 with a test, except the four in 245.
239. Built, the numbers: a lift more than 4 weeks off comes back at about 90% of its last working load with 2 or more in reserve and its usual sets ("Back after 5 weeks"), then picks up from there (a convention: strength holds for about 4 weeks off, Bosquet 2013); its trend says when it was last done instead of up or down, with no projection and no "PR last time"; no RIR check on it. Bodyweight lifts never done with added load (push-ups, pull-ups) progress by reps, never to BW+10, and are never moved to a load mid-session. Every set at 5 or more in reserve steps up at least once. A dumbbell typed in the other unit is that bell (35 lb is the 16 kg dumbbell). The plates line and the bar follow the gym's unit (one 20 kg plate a side, not lb plates). A heavier load with the reps still in range never reads as down. A weaker set at a heavier load is no PR; a best for its reps is named "Best for 6 to 10 reps", not "Best estimate". Two sessions on one day keep their own PRs. Weight lifted counts leg raises, Nordics and sissy squats at their bodyweight share. Rest weeks are said as rest weeks; weeks off are not "a lighter week". A blank effort mid-session reads as the lift's usual effort.
240. Built, the session: the adjustment line goes when its set is removed, unticked or turned into a warm-up; Fill in suggested loads keeps adjusted rows; a ramp-up set the app suggested is never offered as a warm-up at Finish; a superset rests once the partners are through; minutes left follow each tick and go at the last set; an open RIR strip is never closed by the auto-fold; the first-time paragraph is said once, a short line on later exercises; the low check-in is one line with the reason behind an i; RPE mode shows "@RPE 9" on every header and in Plan; after a unit change the targets and Last line read in the new unit; the heavier-than-target line shows whole pounds, and the set on an isolation lift.

Built, accounts and wording (V: "why is it talking about shared phones anywhere at all"): no line anywhere about shared phones, someone else, or another person, checked by a scan of the source and of every tab, tip, the menu and the Guide (tests/r34b.js, section 3; no em dashes either). Signed out, Your data says once "Saved on this phone only. Create an account to back it up and use it on any device." (it said it four times). Backup due never shows for an account synced this week. Report a problem and the owner will delete it for you."). Buttons say what they are doing (Signing in, Creating your account). A report signed out goes to the app owner. Freestyle Today has More options (rest week, deload, injury, cardio); "Log a workout now" on the first page starts freestyle with no routine talk.
