Built, wording: "Rest (rest)", "On track. ...", "0 week streak", 88 sets said three times, "over your first 4 sets" of 4, the bests board's repeated line, "0 in 14 days" above old PRs, "1 hard sets", targets on tracked-only muscles, the radar's second count, "the last 43 days" on bodyweight (now the dates), "Freestyle freestyle" in History (and a start time on two sessions of one day; the section says since when instead of repeating the count), Empty Recently deleted asks without "for good" beside Undo, the Layout tip, the weigh-in card only for someone who logs bodyweight lifts (with the last weigh-in in grey), the Guide matching each rule.

#### r34, built 2026-10-10 on top of r33 (V's questions and answers of 2026-10-10), live since 2026-10-10; tests/r34.js
221. Built (V: "adjust sets and weights in real time"; best practice, on by default): after a ticked working set that was clearly off, the next sets whose load is still the app's (grey, or filled in by "Fill in suggested loads") move. Up: 2 or more reps in reserve over the target with the reps in range, or 3 or more past the top. Down: 2 or more under both the range and what the session expected of that set, not stopped early. The new load is what that set's estimate allows mid-range (up, at most 10%) or at the bottom (down) at the target RIR plus one for fatigue, in the lift's steps or on the rack. A line says why, with Keep. Off for warm-ups, drops, timed, assisted, fixed loads, injuries, lighter days, deloads, past workouts, edits, and in Settings. The Guide states the rule. tests/realism.js's 16-week lifter rates effort per set and triggers it 44 times (grey) and 50 (filled) with every progression check holding; tests/progress.js checks the cross-week rules with it off.
222. Built (V): the first-page tour tightened to five titles naming what the app does (Log a set in one tap, Next weights worked out, Adjusts as you lift, Your plan or freestyle, See every lift progress), smaller pictures, no line under each, one pass then it stops. The calendar and volume slides left; both stay in the app.
223. Built (V): the Exercise library button has no line under it. Before anything is logged, stats show a dash instead of 0 (ring, At a glance, Stats tiles and subtitles, History, This week, the muscle map's legend and tips); a real 0 once there is a log stays a number.
224. Built (V): bodyweight is asked (optional) on the Set up page, then a Weigh-in due card on Today every 2 weeks (Settings > General: every week, 2 weeks, month, or off; Later waits 2 days). Bodyweight lifts are now read at the latest weigh-in everywhere (estimates, trends, charts, stalls, the lighter-week check, the load engine), as PRs were since r32.1; weight lifted keeps each day's. Found on a reviewer's lifter: unchanged push-ups read 10.6% weaker after 8 kg lost, with "look at recovery".
225. Built (V): Settings > Rest timer and logging > Today starts with: my plan's day or a blank session. In freestyle mode the week is measured against the lifter's own training: the streak counts weeks trained, This week says sessions and hard sets, adherence has no plan. Each lift still gets its suggestion from history.
226. Built (V): two-a-days. Sessions store when they started (`t`) and are ordered by it within a day everywhere (they were ordered by random id after a sync); a calendar day with two or more asks which; a week counts sessions, not days.
227. Built (V): Rest week (Today > More options): this week or last, not counted as missed (averages reach past it, streak and adherence skip it, the coach reads it as recovery), with Undo.
228. Built (V): + Drop beside + Set once a set is done. Suggest for the library on a lifter's own exercise sends it to the owner as a suggestion (needs B5 to arrive as a ticket); exercises a lifter adds stay in their own log.
230. Done 2026-10-11, see 238 (was: to do after the weekly usage limit resets (1am), on the live app once r34 is published (V, 2026-10-10: "independent reviewer can restart after 1 am on latest live app"): the independent end-to-end review of r34 across lifter types (a new user, a plan follower in lb with supersets, drops, deload, rest week and RPE, a freestyler in kg with two-a-days and a dumbbell rack, a calisthenics lifter). The first attempt was cut off by the limit after 30 minutes; its screenshots led to 224 and 225. Also to check: a lat pulldown reading "slowly down" after 90 x 14 to 95 x 10 (honest by rep-max tables, to confirm).)
231. Built (V: "reducing the overwhelming subtext ... into quick actionable info boxes"; the screen-by-screen pass of item 99, started): every tab read with demo data and every section open, and the wordy lines cut to the number and the action with the reasons behind an i: the Coach (and the Change row on Stats) is one line, e.g. "Traps: 1 hard set a week, target 6-16. Add an exercise for it. 3 more below target.", with Why; a lift's headline on Stats is one line (verdict, bodyweight ratio, 4-week projection) with the rate, ranges and sessions behind an i, since the tiles under it show the numbers; Muscles rows are one short line ("1 hard set", not "1 hard sets"); Strength return on volume says "keep the sets as they are" once instead of "Rising: hold this volume" on every row; Size check states the dates, waist and bodyweight once above the rows; the radar says which weeks it uses with how to read it behind an i, and no longer repeats the table's lists; Settings: RIR in one line, Layout's and Help's filler gone. Estimates (1RM, projections, PR toasts, the chart, the bests board) show whole pounds or half kilos: "294 → 293.8 → 301" read as precision the numbers do not have; loads keep their decimals. Left for V's notes: the Guide (long on purpose), the sheets.

Supersets checked end to end (V asked whether they are easy and in the math): two taps (the exercise's ⋯, Superset with the next one) in a session, Superset with next in Plan, the pair named on the exercise with when to rest, no rest timer between partners, session minutes without that rest, adjusting per exercise inside a pair, Unlink in the same menu, kept through a reload. No new button: two taps is how Strong and Hevy do it, and V asked "if it's already easy do we need it?". Tested in tests/r34.js (section 5); before, only splitting a pair by a move was tested.
233. Built: the installed app serves its own copies of Chart.js, SortableJS, three.js, supabase-js (with its bundled packages), and two fonts, whose MIT and SIL Open Font licenses ask for their notice with every copy. The build writes LICENSES.txt with each license in full (and fails if one is missing); Settings > Diagnostics links it in the installed app. Checked by tests/pwa.js.

#### r33, built 2026-10-09 and 10 (V: "go ahead with those", with V's feedback from using it), live with r34 since 2026-10-10; tests/r33.js
206. Built: effort entered as whole RIR (the default, nothing stored), half reps, or RPE (Settings > Effort, `settings.rirScale`). Stored as RIR either way (RPE 8.5 is RIR 1.5), so every estimate, hard-set count and target reads it unchanged and switching loses nothing. Half points and RPE are a grid under the label, 40 px or more a button at 320 px. History lines read @RPE 8.5. Type sets reads @ and "rpe" as RPE in RPE mode.
207. Built: Settings > Your gym (`settings.gym`, absent unless set): the bar weight and small plates (moved here from General), plates on hand, dumbbells (From, To, Step, and smaller steps at the light end), kettlebells, the cable and machine stack step, and the unit the gym is labelled in. Dumbbell and kettlebell suggestions, deloads, lighter days, warm-ups, drops and a close variation's start land on the rack; the top of the rack is the heaviest available (more reps, and not a stall); a lift logged above it steps on by the rack's spacing. A barbell or a two-sleeve plate machine steps by two of the smallest plate on hand, a landmine by one; stacks by the stack step. A step set on the exercise or in the plan still wins. With nothing set, all suggestions match r32.1 word for word (tests/r33.js, 165 histories; the reviewer checked 1,400 more in lb and kg).
214. Built (V): Sign out works like any app with an account. Sign out is the main button (the log is in the account; signing in brings it back) and removes the phone's copy only once every change is in the account; if not, it offers to keep them on the phone. "Sign out, keep a copy on this phone" is a quieter link. Then the first page (per device, `KEY+'.signedOut'`); Continue without an account opens the kept copy. A session in progress kept on the phone carries on. No shared-phone wording.
215. Built (V): the first page's three tiles became a tour: Log a set in one tap, Pick a plan or build one, Track every lift, Every session, by day, Volume by muscle, each with a small picture of that screen (sample numbers in the unit chosen). Swipe or tap a dot; it moves on every 5.5 s until touched, never with reduced motion. The page still fits 390 x 844.
216. Built (V: 53 marked Common was too many to help): Most common is the 30 lifts in the top 25 by sets logged, overall, for men, or for women, among over 700,000 StrengthLog users (2025; https://www.strengthlog.com/most-popular-exercises/), measured rather than picked. Less common is unchanged.
217. Library QC (V): all 174 exercises checked (unique ids and names, real muscles and equipment, bodyweight and assisted flags, notes, every ready-made routine's lifts); clean. Kept as a check in tests/r33.js.
218. The independent review of r33 (2026-10-10, a fresh reviewer with no history): 9 findings and 6 small ones, all fixed with tests (tests/r33.js, Part 1e): a rack in the other unit read a bell typed as shown as just under it and said "add 0"; two-sleeve plate machines stepped by one plate; signing out mid-session hid the tabs; RPE was read as RIR in typed sets; the top of the rack read as a stall; a lift above the rack was called the heaviest available; the step sheet ignored a typed number equal to the one shown; the gym's unit could not be chosen; the half-point buttons were 22 px wide; then the dots' size, the log picture, the Most common tip, racks over 300 weights, and the signed-out flag after a confirmation link. One of its notes was wrong and was not changed: the October picture already started on a Thursday. Also found while building, by tests/r22.js: the tour's pictures shared the rest timer's class and stretched it; renamed, with a check against r32.1.

Cloud merge (found by the Undo of Clear my gym): a setting absent from the merge base is now read as absent, so a setting added on one phone, or brought back by Undo, is not lost to the other side's absence. r32.1 phones still merge the old way: once r33 is live, update every phone (OWNER.md A15).

#### r32.1, live 2026-10-09 to 10 (replaced by r34); tests/r32b.js
211. Bodyweight lifts no longer get a PR from the scale: every set's load includes that day's bodyweight, so a weigh-in 0.4 kg up made the same 8 pull-ups or 12 leg raises a counted PR (8 identical weeks with weigh-ins rising: 10 PRs; the bests board dated the record to the heaviest day). PRs on a bodyweight lift are now judged at the latest weigh-in for every session (`prLoad`): more reps or more added load, as lifters count them. The PR list, the count, the live mark and toast, the bests board's records and heaviest set all use it; estimates, trends and charts still use each day's bodyweight. Falling weigh-ins: the same PRs, none lost. Loaded lifts unchanged.
212. A close variation's first session starts from the related lift (r32's second check: DB curl to hammer curl started from nothing): the newest lift trained in the last 120 days with the same equipment (free weights only: barbell, dumbbell, kettlebell, landmine), load meaning, sides, main muscle and movement, not one leg or arm at a time (`kinOf`, `kinStart`). About 90% of its estimate (80% on a compound lift), for the middle of the range with one more rep in reserve, rounded down to the step or the related lift's rack; the line names the lift and set it came from. Conventions, meant to err light. Machines, cables, lunges from squats, dumbbells from a barbell, and a related lift last done over 120 days ago still start fresh. Checked by hand: hammer curl 14 kg from DB curl 16 x 10; front squat 92.5 from back squat 120 x 8; incline bench 75 from bench 100 x 8; 30 lb from 35 lb on a 5 lb rack.
213. Housekeeping: r32 saved as `baselines/r32.html` and added to the migration suite; OWNER.md's list and D6's branch list brought up to date (14 merged branches).

#### r32, live since 2026-10-07 (V: "implement 1 and 2 and 3"); tests/r32.js has every item with the reviewer's inputs
Built: 193 to 205 and 208. A second fresh reviewer then used this build (14 sessions, a Strong import, engine sweeps): items 1 to 3, 5 to 8, 10 and 11 fixed, 4 and 9 partly. Its new findings, also fixed in r32 with tests (tests/r32.js, part 4): 5 x 5 at beginner loads asked for reps past 5 (a fixed-rep plan now always steps); one good day made a squat stalled (the stall line is now the median of pairwise slopes); a weaker set later in a session was announced as a PR; Lighter-day sets were listed as PRs; "Bench 100 3x8 @9" imported 9 kg sets (a bare @6 to @10 with a load on the line is RPE); one bad session set the target and four weeks under the range kept the load (a bad day now asks for the range again, four sessions under it drop the load, and the one-set floor is said); trend words from 3 sessions in a week (none now); a kg log shown in lb suggested 225.5 lb (suggestions now snap to the step on screen); 100 x 105 saved as a Rep PR (odd reps ask first); the PR toast now says "estimated 1RM ... kg"; Recent PRs lists only counted PRs; Short on time's minutes agree; Change day fits 320 px; "Lat raise" matches DB Lateral Raise. Left for later, now built in r32.1: a swap to a close variation starting from nothing (212); rep PRs on bodyweight lifts every session (211).
206 and 207 built in r33. 209 done in r36 (254 and 255).
The third independent review (2026-10-06, a fresh reviewer with no history, 20 sessions through the real app plus a 780-row Strong import; report kept in this item list) found that the logging and data handling hold up (backup, restore, CSV, Undo, reload, swaps, plates, barbell and stack progression all checked), but the suggested loads and some readings are still not trustworthy on sight. Its findings, checked in the code, and V's and Claude's items from the same day, by priority:

##### Priority 1, r32: the load engine, rebuilt so the laughable numbers cannot happen (one pull request)
193. Built. A Lighter day or deload can be heavier than the normal day: after squat 140 x 4, 3, 3 the normal day asks 122.5 kg, the Lighter day "about 125 kg for 6-10 with 3 or more in reserve", and grey reps keep last time's reps (90 x 15 logged with one tap on a "3 or more in reserve" day). Cause: `suggest()` takes 90% of last time's top load (`top`), not the corrected target, and keeps `lastR`. Fix: lighter and deload loads are fractions of the target the normal day would set, in whole steps, and grey reps follow that day's range and reps in reserve.
194. Built, by what the logged loads fit (a 2 kg or 2.5 kg rack, 4 kg kettlebells, 5 lb), not a fixed 2 kg default. Dumbbells that do not exist: 12 to 14.5 kg, 34 to 36.5, 9.5 and 11.5 on a lighter day. Cause: the step is added to whatever was typed, and nothing knows what a rack holds. Fix: dumbbell lifts step 2 kg or 5 lb by default (a setting for racks in 2.5 kg), and every suggestion lands on a weight that is a whole step from zero (or from the stack's first plate). Later, a list of the weights each piece of equipment has (item 204).
195. Built. One bad set becomes next time's target: squat 140 x 3, 6, 6 (range 6-10) gives grey reps (3)(6)(6) and "16+ reps over your first 3 sets". Fix: a set far under the session's other working sets does not set grey reps, and grey reps never sit below the plan's minimum unless the whole session did.
196. Built: a step costing 1.5 reps or more is built up to first (reps past the top, up to a quarter more), so no target sits under the range after an ordinary step and a 5 x 5 still goes up 5 lb. Targets under the planned range after a load step (cable fly 20 kg "for 9+" on 10-15, incline curl "8+" on 10-15). Mostly a result of 194's half-real steps; after 194, below-range targets stay only after a step that is large for the load (r31.1, item 188) and always say "then build back to" the range.
197. Built as a wrapper and helpers in place (suggestCore, rackStep, repFloor, the build-up), not yet a separate module (that is 209). The side-by-side check is in tests/r32.js: 3,276 histories against r31.3, every difference one of 193 to 196 or 203. Planned as:  suggest(), the lighter and deload paths, grey reps (`greyR`, `lastOf`), `workLoad`, the large-step aim and the step rules become one module with one input (the log, the plan, the equipment) and one output (load, reps per set, target), unit-tested on its own. Before it replaces the old code, both run side by side on thousands of simulated logs (tests/realism.js's lifter and the review's inputs); every difference is either one of 193 to 196 or a bug. Stored data does not change shape.

##### Priority 2, r32.1: the rest of the review
198. Built. "Over 6 weeks" is still fixed text in three places (the Moving line on Stats, the muscle strength line, the coach's "down X% over 6 weeks"); item 181 fixed the lift lines only. Two weeks of data read "+7.5% over 6 weeks", and "Chest +4%, rising" after 3 sessions. Fix: every trend line says the span it rests on, and a muscle trend needs the same evidence as a lift.
199. Built: a workout that comes round twice or more a week repeats in the cycle (push, pull, legs, push, pull, legs, rest; upper, lower, rest, upper, lower, rest, rest). A Strong import of push, pull, legs twice a week (PPLPPL, rest) becomes "3 training days in a 4-day cycle", Today says "0 of ~5 sessions", adherence 21/21 for 24 sessions. Fix: `routineFromLog` reads how often each workout came round in a week; adherence counts every session.
200. Built: a stall needs the newest session to beat every earlier one at its load, a noisy slope reads as flat, the lift's trend word follows its trend (the press reads stalled, never up), and at the top of a range the Coach says it is due a step up. The curls (14 x 10 to 14 x 11, then flat for 3 training weeks around a deload) still read stalled: that is the rule (flat for 3 weeks); a reset after each deload was tried and undone, since it hid the r30 press that really stalls. Trends the wrong way on imported data: OHP at 60 kg for 7 weeks (6, 6, 6 then 5, 5, 4) reads "up" and is not stalled, while curls 14 x 10 to 14 x 11 are listed as stalled (item 180 says that is progress). The coach tells someone at the top of 12-15 to try another rep range instead of adding load.
201. Built. Notes import: "Deadlift 180x5 @RPE8" dropped; "Chins +10 3x8" refused for no weight (the +10 is the weight) and guessed as Chest; "Pull-ups BW 3x10" becomes a new exercise instead of Pull-up.
202. Built: one PR per lift per session was already the rule; a PR is now counted and announced only when the set is stronger than every earlier one (102.5 x 7 after 100 x 10 is listed and named in the recap as heaviest yet, not counted). This narrows V's r29 weight PR (item 135): a heavier set with far fewer reps is no longer a gold toast. PRs as noise: "8 NEW PRS" in one session, "34 PRs" in 30 days after an import. Check why more than one per lift per session got through; small weight steps stop being a headline PR unless the estimate rose too.
203. Built (ab work on bodyweight only; a pull-up still adds load, as programs do). Bodyweight core lifts: hanging leg raise at 15 reps is told "BW+2.5 for 10+". Lifts with no added load ever logged progress by reps (or time) up to a cap, then suggest adding load once.
204. Built: minutes in fives on Today; one bar after Continue without an account (the account line returns after a first session); the toast reads "Weekly set targets now match it"; an RIR check passed over waits 3 weeks; small numbers in the body font. Polish: the routine picker's "55 to 75 min" and Today's "about 77 min" must agree; the first Today after Continue without an account shows one bar at a time, and the routine toast drops "weekly minimums"; "Due for an RIR check" about once every 4 weeks per lift, not every session; a display font whose 1 does not read as I.

##### Priority 3: gaps a Strong or Hevy user expects on day one
205. Built: History and chart in the ⋯ menu (the last 10 sessions as logged, a strength line, the best, Open in Stats). An exercise's history and chart from inside a session (the ⋯ menu), not only the Last line.
206. Built in r33 (see above).
207. Built in r33 (see above).
208. Built: the first session with a bodyweight lift and no weigh-in asks once (Save, Not now). Pull-ups and leg raises count in weight lifted only after a weigh-in; ask for bodyweight once when the first bodyweight lift is logged.

##### Priority 4: the code, rebuilt for the long run (no visible change)
209. Split the source into modules (storage and sync, math, analytics, the load engine from 197, screens); `scripts/build.js` joins them into the one file the artifact and the phone app need. Done step by step, every suite passing after each step. Then smaller functions where the rules piled up round after round (`IDX()` is 356 lines, `suggest()` 194), each with unit tests.

##### Priority 5: owner decisions and features
192 (privacy policy page before going beyond friends), 189 (a lawyer's look), 164 (percentage programs: 5/3/1, nSuns, GZCLP; a training max per lift, a week-by-week table of percentages, AMRAP sets, the max rising each cycle, and planned light weeks never read as a deload or a fall; about one build once 197 is in), 188 (lift goals: built in r38, item 258), 210 (Coach: hidden on Today by default since r28, never removed; V thought it was gone. V's call whether a new install shows it), 191 (swipeable first page), 141 (female body, redrawn flat body), 46 (rest timer alerts through a locked iPhone need the native wrapper), 129 (private repository).

#### r31 (live since 2026-10-05) and r31.1 (pull request #16): the second independent review, each finding fixed with the reviewer's own inputs as a test (tests/r31.js)
175. A top single before back-off sets (405 x 1, then 335 x 5 x 5) no longer becomes the working set: targets, grey reps and trends build on the working load (the load done for the most sets, among loads at 60% or more of the heaviest total moved, so +25 back-offs on a 180 lb pull-up count against +50); grey reps are last time's reps at that load only (a reverse pyramid 225 x 7, 205 x 8, 185 x 10 no longer asks 225 x 10); a top single, a pair of singles or triples, or one heavy top set before back-offs (365 x 4 then 335 x 5 x 5) still counts for bests and PRs and is never prescribed for every set. Squat now asks 340, bench stays at 230 to beat the reps.
176. An unmarked deload on a lift trained 3 times a week is read as lighter: a new level needs lighter sessions in a row for 2 weeks or more, not three sessions.
177. Make it lighter changes only the exercises not started; work already done at full effort still sets next time's target and counts in trends. No "take a set to failure" after two low check-in answers, a lighter day or a deload.
178. Load-finding in higher rep ranges: for picking a load only, sets past 12 reps to failure are read with Epley up to 30, and the first 3 sessions of a lift can move its load up to 25% (a convention). Leg press 3 x 25 at 90 lb now asks 110, and 3 x 12 at RIR 5 asks 100 on a 1 RIR plan (95 on the beginner plan's 3 RIR).
179. A first-run import (Strong, Hevy, a sheet) makes its routine from the file: the workouts' names are the days (or, with Strong's default names such as Morning Workout, the sessions grouped by their lifts into Day A, Day B), in the order they came round, with rest days for how often they trained, each day's lifts, sets, range and RIR (only when most sets were rated) from its latest 3 full sessions (so a program changed a few weeks ago is the program now, and deload weeks are left out); the sessions join their days; weekly minimums fit it and never sit above what the log itself averaged, also after Log a workout now; a note says so (Undo). When no day repeats, the routine picker opens and says why. Adherence shows whole counts. No more 69% adherence or "add sets to Lat Pulldown" against a routine never picked.
180. Double progression at the top of the range is progress: trends read the average of the working sets, not the best one set, and more reps at the same load over no more sets is not a stall. Flat is under 0.1% a week, so an identical lift is always stalled (averaging left rounding dust that hid it).
181. A trend needs 3 sessions spread over 2 weeks or more, and says the weeks it covers ("over 3 weeks"), never a fixed "over 6 weeks" (on the lift lines; three other lines kept it, see 198). A small rise the interval puts above zero reads "slowly up", not "no clear change".
182. A set ticked with no load is asked about as a missing load, never as a ramp-up set.
183. The first-time line asks for one load: "a load you think you could lift about 15 times, erring light; 8 to 12 reps with about 3 still in you" (the two now agree); bodyweight and assisted lifts get their own wording.
184. Weekly averages say when weeks were missed ("the 1 week you trained of the last 4"), and with 2 or more of 4 missed the coach says that first.
185. Only main lifts (compound, with an estimate) count toward "lifts stalled at once, a deload week may help"; planks, curls and leg raises are listed as stalled but not counted.
190. r31.2 (V, 2026-10-06; a friend stopped at the first page): the first page now shows three things the app does side by side, an icon and a few words each (Log sets with a tap, Build your plan, See your progress), then Save your log with one short line on what the account is for, and Continue without an account as a plain link. The sign-up sheet, the privacy tips and the Guide say what the email is for and who can see it. The By region radar averages the last 2, 4 or 8 full weeks (4 unless changed, kept per phone); it never shows one week unless no full week is logged yet, and then says so. The spreadsheet export now imports back with its workout names and deload marks (it lost both), and its tip says so. Average labels say the span and any lighter week left out (an independent check found "3 full weeks logged so far" on a 9-week log with a deload and a missed week). Tested on the real build with the stand-in server, and at 320 to 390 px in both themes.
188. r31.1 (V, 2026-10-05: "suggested weights being a joke or not progressing realistically"): tests/realism.js, a simulated lifter following the app for 16 weeks with grey and filled-in loads (true strength growing or flat, noise, a deload, a cautious first guess). It found one real problem, now fixed: after a step that is large for the load (35 to 40 lb dumbbells, 14%), the app asked for the bottom of the range, which the lifter could not reach; the target is now what the estimate allows ("40 for 7+, then build back to 12") and the load no longer swings back down while reps rise. Library search ignores hyphens and spaces ("t bar row" now finds Landmine T-Bar Row). Lift goals (a target weight for a lift, with progress toward it) do not exist yet; only a bodyweight goal does. Candidate for a later build.
186. Polish: "5×5" and "Do 5 reps" (never "5×5-5" or "5 to 5"); "1 rep"; the routine note no longer covers Discard after Start session; the grey-loads card only when something is grey; the region table shows each region's minimum (not "14-66"); an import repeated with the other unit skipped as duplicates; one shared test for lighter sessions (it was written out in six places); the coach names a lift you do.

#### r30, live since 2026-10-04 (V's go): the independent review (Ironlog_feedback.docx); move to Done once V has used it
V's decisions for r30 (2026-10-04):
- Weekly targets: keep the r29 fit to the chosen routine (never back to "below target everywhere"); Plan > Weekly volume adds one quiet line naming muscles the plan puts under the usual 10 sets; the first-run toast says the targets match the plan, not that everything is on target.
- Progressive overload must be applied visibly in the suggested loads and reps over time, overridable per exercise in the Plan editor; nothing called stalled or behind for not moving fast every session or week.
- Demo: keep it progressing and on track, add one marked deload week and one missed day. No stalls.
- Repository privacy (item 129): decide later; r30 changes nothing about hosting.

What r30 changes (each with a test in tests/r30.js, reproduced the way the reviewer met it):
148. Training blocks: a session nobody marked as a deload (an import, say) is read from its numbers as lighter (load 10%+ under its last three and strength reading 7%+ under), and a week where most lifts read lighter is a lighter week. They stay in history and charts and are left out of trends, stalls, targets and the weekly volume average, like a marked deload. Three lighter sessions in a row count as a new level instead.
149. Trends that do not mislead: RIR is used in a lift's trend only when it is rated on 80% or more of its sets (else loads and reps alone, the same way every session), so patchy RPE cannot turn 245 to 270 into a stall; trends read up to 20 reps (leg press for 15 has one); each lift's 6 weeks end at its own last session; a trend shows how many sessions it rests on and its weekly rate with a likely range; a muscle with nothing outside the noise reads unclear, never +0%, and gets no "try 2 more sets"; "needs 3 sessions" is said only when true.
150. Stalls only when real (V: "not stalled or behind if not moving at a crazy pace"): flat 3 weeks or more, and no PR in the last 3 sessions, and the load no heavier than at the start. No deload suggested within 4 weeks of a lighter week. A rising muscle is progressing whatever its rank.
151. Numbers fixed: weight lifted is a number, never "-5% vs last week"; a rep PR counts at 90% or more of the strongest set before it (no "40 x 10 after 95 x 5", no 54 PRs in 14 days); all-time bests carry their own dates; the PR toast names the rep band its estimate beat; a 1RM only on compound lifts (isolation lifts show their best set); "301 → 300.7 · up" reads flat; a stalled lift never also says PR last time; a rest day says the card is the next day; a weigh-in today never changes past weight lifted; no session-length average from one session.
152. Progressive overload you can set: Plan's exercise editor has Load jump % (blank: 2.5% compound, 5% isolation; 0: one step every time). A history in another rep range (an import at 5 x 5 under a 3 x 6-10 plan) starts from the estimate for the new range, and rep targets count the plan's sets.
153. Lighter day is lighter: half the sets, about 90% loads, 3+ RIR, nothing to beat. The check-in now acts: two or more low answers offer Make it lighter and Short on time.
154. Finish: asks when sets are left; an exercise stopped with under half its sets done is saved as trimmed (it never sets the next target); the recap counts targets only for exercises started and planned minutes after any trimming.
155. Demo data never mixes with a real log: saving a real session removes it and the recap says so. The demo has one marked deload week and one missed day (V's choice), still nothing stalled or below target.
156. Import: "OHP 3x8 40" is 3 sets of 8 at 40; Strong treadmill and other cardio rows become cardio entries with time and distance; "Triceps Pushdown (Cable - Straight Bar)" and rope variants match. Cardio has a distance field (km, or miles when logging in lb).
157. Plan editor: a rep minimum typed above the maximum raises it (never "12-10").
158. Swaps: Nordic curls, pull-ups, chin-ups, dips, and other hard bodyweight lifts are not picked for someone who has not done them; when one is the only match it gets 3 to 6 reps at 2 RIR and a note. The Limited equipment chips no longer move under the finger.
159. Injuries by joint: knee, hip, shoulder, elbow, wrist, each marking the exercises that load it.
160. Small: Enter fills Type sets; the kg example is in kg; cancelling an untouched edit closes without "Discard this session?".
161. Routines: the beginner full body is 5 exercises a day on machines and dumbbells at 3 RIR (about 45 minutes, with start-light guidance); new Full body, 2 days and Strength, 5 x 5; the 5-day split's lateral raises spread over two days; the high-volume template at most 11 sets per muscle per session, without the neck circuit. Your own routines are unchanged (item 39 still yours to decide). New lifts: sumo deadlift, pause squat, paused bench, floor press, power clean, kettlebell swing.
162. Weekly targets (V's choice): kept fitted to the routine; Plan, Weekly volume adds one quiet line naming muscles that plan puts under the usual range; the toast says minimums were set to the plan. Nothing new on Stats or Today.
174. r30.2, a consistency pass V asked for ("is the logic change reflected across all associated functions"): every place that reads each r30 rule was listed and checked. Fixed: the Lifts section showed e1RM tiles, a column and a chart for isolation lifts while the headline gave the best set (now one rule: 1RM figures only for a compound lift with sets of 12 or fewer; otherwise best set and a top-set-load chart); leg press for 15 reps had a trend but no chart and an "n/a" tile (now charted by top-set load); deload and lighter sessions were an unexplained dip in the Lifts table (now tagged); the chart's highest point is the tile's best estimate (checked by a test); isolation PRs were shown as a 1RM number in the PR list, recap and toast (now the set); Pick for me ignored a flagged joint when ranking days (now counted, with a reason). Checked and consistent: lighter sessions in every reader, the lighter day in every block builder, the load jump in every progression path, rep PRs in every display.
173. r30.1, from an audit V asked for ("no inaccurate math or nonsensical trends"): a messy six-week log (unmarked deload, missed day, stalling press, patchy RPE) recomputed independently, outside the app, from the documented rules: 226 numbers compared (each lift's best estimate, 6-week change, weekly rate and its range, moving, RIR use, sessions read as lighter; hard sets per muscle per week; weight lifted per session; the 4-week average). Three differed, two from real flaws, both fixed: a lift done at its usual load in a deload week was called lighter (the week rule now needs that lift lighter too), and perfectly steady numbers printed a range of zero width ("likely +1.9% to +1.9%"). Reading Stats as a person then found two more: Stalls said "No lighter week in the last 12" beside a deload week of lighter loads (it counted sets only), and Moving named two lifts when five were rising (it now says "and 3 more"). After the fixes, all 226 match.
170. Second pass over the review (V asked "are you sure you went through every piece", 2026-10-04): a first session now says how to pick a load in plain words (a load you could lift about 5 more times than the top of the range, stopping with the reps in reserve shown) and the workout preview says once to warm up on the first big lift; the import preview flags lines that look misread (a weighted lift under 5 lb, or over 30 reps); the setup page counts the plans itself (it still said "Six plans, 3 to 6 days"); the rest-day card is tested. Found on the way, from before r30: with auto-mark on, typing reps and then tapping the same row's RIR box near the bottom of the screen ticked the set at once and the rest timer bar rose under the finger, so the RIR strip never opened; auto-mark now waits until the strip closes.
163. How-to videos: an exercise's menu and editor open a YouTube search for it (only the name leaves the app, and only when tapped).

#### r29.5 (live since 2026-10-04), was waiting for V's go (V, 2026-10-04)
144. Common lifts marked, not only the less common ones ("marking most common is more helpful"): 53 staples found in most gyms and programs carry a Common tag in the library, the exercise picker, and Plan's Add exercises list; they come first in the picker (Common, then All other exercises; with a muscle chosen or a search, common matches first) and in Plan's list; the library filters All, Common, and Less common, with an i saying Common is a judgment, not a measurement. Nothing is stored: it follows the library id, and a staple the lifter marks as less common reads Less common only.
145. Why the target RIR is 1 (a friend's question): an i beside Target RIR in the Plan editor and a line in the Guide (Plan topic) say it is a convention: growth improves as sets end closer to failure (Robinson 2024), but failure grew muscle no more than stopping 1 to 2 reps short (Refalo 2024) and costs more fatigue. Some ready-made lifts use 0 or 2. No number changed.
146. The installed app leaves out the Claude-only features (debrief, Ask Claude, suggested swaps, Claude reading typed sets), which only ever worked inside the Claude artifact, so their prompts no longer ship to every phone (a friend read them in the page source). The source marks each block and `scripts/build.js` drops them; the source keeps them for the artifact. Type or dictate sets still works and states the pattern when it cannot read the text. What still mentions Claude in the installed app: the storage path and privacy wording for the artifact, which never runs there.
147. Housekeeping: the six merged branches (keep-domain, r26 to r29.4) are V's to delete (OWNER.md D6; Claude's access refuses branch deletion), all already in main; OWNER.md's Now list rewritten (B6, encryption, the phone checks); r29.4 saved as `baselines/r29.html` and added to the migration suite.

#### Built in r29, live since 2026-10-03 as r29.3 (V's requests of 2026-10-02 evening and 2026-10-03); move to Done once V has used it
117. Volume, By region: reads only what you logged (your average, else this week so far, with a line that the week is still going), never the routine's plan; with nothing logged it says so. Muscles no longer has a note about the plan. (r28 had shown the plan until a full week was logged, which made every new person look behind.)
118. Neck and tibialis are tracked only, like serratus and rotator cuff: counted and shown, no target, never flagged, and left out of their region's target on the radar. No ready-made routine trains them, so as targets they made every routine read as short. A convention, stated in the Guide and in Weekly set targets. (Was decision 115.)
119. The serial (Oxford) comma in every list in a sentence on screen, in tips and in the Guide, and in lists the app builds (`andList`).
120. A slimmer, athletic 3D body: the torso tapers from the chest to a narrower waist, with flatter muscle shapes; every muscle still shaded and tappable.
121. During a session, each exercise has a ⠿ handle: drag it to reorder, or use the arrow keys on it; the ⋯ menu's Move up and Move down still work. Done exercises stay done and folded, typed sets move with their exercise. From the r29 review: tapping the handle never ticks a set, a move that splits a superset clears it, and the handle takes a 44 px touch at 320 px.
122. OWNER.md D5: a security checklist for every account (email, GitHub, Supabase, Cloudflare, Resend, Claude's GitHub access).
123. From the r29 review: the week a log started in is averaged only if it started on that week's first day. Someone who began on a Saturday read as a "1-week average" with most muscles flagged the following Monday.
124. From the r29 review: on a first run, picking a ready-made routine brings each weekly minimum down to what that routine plans (whole sets), so following it is on target; the toast says so and Settings raises them. Only when the targets are still the defaults; tracked-only muscles and the maximums are unchanged. Tracked-only muscles are also left out of Pick for me's deficits, the day ranking, the weekly trend's target lines, and the volume rows' zones.
125. Security, from the r29 audit (V asked for it before merging): the owner's Reports inbox showed a report's screenshot after checking only its start, so a report sent straight to the API could run script on V's page when he opened the inbox (not reachable yet: the reports table is not created until OWNER.md B1). The app now shows only a plain base64 JPEG, escaped; the B1 SQL refuses anything else, and the trigger sets each report's time and ticket link itself, so a back-dated report no longer slips past the 5-a-day limit and a sender cannot plant a ticket link. Tested on Postgres (tests/sql.js) and in the app (tests/accounts.js, which fails on the old code).
126. From the r29 audit: Undo of an earlier action that did not change the session's order (a Settings change, Default layout) put back the old exercise order after a drag. Sets were never lost. The order now stays.
127. From the r29 audit: dragging an exercise could only move it one place, because an open exercise is taller than the screen and the page did not scroll during a drag. While dragging, every exercise now shows one line, and the page scrolls when the finger is near the top or bottom.
131. Decision 114 (V, 2026-10-03): a new install starts on Upper / lower, 4 days, not the six-day high-volume split. Log a workout now keeps it with targets fitted to it (and says so); picking another routine on the first pages replaces it, as it does the old starter on an install from before r29. Existing logs, V's included, keep their routines.
132. V, 2026-10-03: the Muscle map showed green before a first set. A routine that does not train a muscle brings its minimum to 0, and 0 of 0 read as on target. No sets is now grey (Not yet) whatever the target, on the map, the 3D body and the volume rows.
133. V, 2026-10-03, "annoying if I am below target every time": the radar and its table measure each region against its minimum, so 100% is on target (it was the middle of the range, so a region at its minimum sat inside the ring); the headline counts regions on target and names the furthest short in sets; the week in progress shows short of the minimum as under way (pale green), not amber; below and above are judged at the one decimal shown (9.96 is not under 10); Muscles leads with the average a flag is judged on, then this week (the line led with this week beside a flag from the average), with one Below target tag; Stats has no Planned chip (the plan is Weekly volume on Plan); a flat muscle is lagging only when one of its lifts has stalled (by rank alone, high-rep work with no estimate read as lagging). The radar draws at most 200% so a small minimum cannot shrink the rest.
134. V, 2026-10-03, "the demo feels shitty and demotivates users": the demo is a lifter following the routine's own double progression, every planned session done, today left open, loads on real steps and never under the bar. Checked on all six ready-made routines, 21 dates, lb and kg: nothing stalled, falling, lagging, or below target; lifts moving; the coach says on track. A first-run demo fits targets to the routine and puts them back when cleared.
135. V, 2026-10-03: a set that was both heavier than ever and a rep record showed only as a rep PR. A weight PR (more weight than any earlier set; less help on an assisted lift) now comes first, then a best estimate, then a rep PR, in the live toast (with what it beat and any new best estimate), the PR list, the chart, and the recap. The Guide now separates the suggested load (today's target), the estimated 1RM (a gauge, never a load to try), and the three kinds of PR.
136. V, 2026-10-03: the rest timer blended in. It is now a deep tint of the accent in both themes, white text at 4.66:1 or more on every accent, with a bar along the top that empties as the rest runs down (kept across a reload); Done is the full accent.
137. Item 116, first half: Finish with a set ticked and no weight asks "Add the missing loads?" first; Add loads goes to the first one, opened, outlined, cursor in it; Save anyway still saves. (Second half, the map early in a week, is 133.)
138. Item 98: Settings has Small plates (1.25 lb or 0.5 kg) for the plates line, and a barbell lift can have its own bar (trap, EZ) in its editor, used for the plates and so a deload never goes under it. A phone still on r28 drops a lift's own bar when it syncs (update every device, as with item 66).
139. Item 128: the installed app and the preview carry a Content Security Policy. Scripts run only from this site and the app's own inline scripts (by hash); data goes only to this site, the Supabase project, and Have I Been Pwned; no plugins, frames, or base tag. Tested: the app, the 3D body, charts, a session, sign-in and sync run with no violation; an injected script does not run and a fetch to another site is refused.
142. V, 2026-10-03, "the muscle guy still looks fat": the 3D body gets athletic proportions: shoulders well outside the ribs, a narrow waist and pelvis, a flatter torso front to back, lean legs that do not touch, slimmer arms, and muscles that sit close on the body. Before and after renders compared; the shape test still holds (waist under the chest and hips). The preview's build name is now 2026.10.03-r29.3, so a phone on an older preview can be told apart.
140. Item 99, the new-person screens only: the repeated Rest day label on rest days in Plan, Stats' "Planned volume works now" line, and Body's three empty lines are gone. The full screen-by-screen pass still waits on V's notes.
130. V, 2026-10-03: the radar showed no chart in the preview. The preview keeps its own log (so testing cannot touch the real one), it had nothing recent, and r29 drew no chart without data. Now the chart always draws (empty against the target ring, or from your last trained weeks after a break, dated), and the preview has Copy my log from Ironlog (Settings > Your data, the first-run pages, the empty radar): it reads your real log from the phone or, on an iPhone, from your account's cloud copy, and never writes to Ironlog. The coach no longer says "after your first full week" to someone back from a break.

#### Before r29
- r29.4 (published 2026-10-03 at V's request, without a preview check):
  143. The first-run account page loses the tagline under the wordmark ("Your lifts, and what to beat next time.") and the line under Save your log ("Free, and on every phone you use."). Spacing kept so the card does not crowd the wordmark. Copy only: no stored data, settings, or behaviour change. Part of item 99's wording pass.
- r28 (approved by V and published 2026-10-02 at https://ironapp.org; merged by Claude at V's go-ahead). V's requests of 2026-10-02 and the review fixes:
  100. The layout a new person starts with, from V's screenshots: every section folded on every tab (Sessions on History excepted, since it is that tab's content); Today shows Workout preview, Muscle map, This week; Plan shows Routine, Add exercises, Days; Stats leads with Lifts. At a glance, Coach and Body on Today and Schedule and Weekly volume on Plan start hidden. Auto-mark starts on. Existing logs, V's included, keep their own layout and settings: the defaults only seed a new install.
  101. Finding the rest without reading first: a tab with hidden sections ends with "Add to this tab" (+ At a glance, + Coach, + Body; one tap shows and opens it) and a link to Settings, Layout; the menu has Show, hide or reorder sections; Settings, Layout has Default layout and Show everything (Simple view is gone, it was a second default). The Guide says all of this.
  102. The menu button is tinted with the accent and ringed, so it stands out from the week bar (kept at 34 px so the days stay 44 px wide on a 375 px phone).
  103. Settings has Help again, near the bottom: Guide, Report a problem, Ask a question, Suggest something (and the Reports inbox for the owner). Still in the menu too.
  104. Stats: the ? left the section headers, where it sat beside the fold arrow; How this works is the first line inside each section. Folds inside a section sit on their own surface, so it is clear they belong to it (they do not move on their own). Inside Lifts: Recent PRs, All-time bests, then Stalls. Volume opens on By region, the radar; with nothing logged yet it reads the routine's plan and says so, instead of a flat zero.
  105. Accuracy: the Lifts explanation said a blank RIR counts as 0. Since r20 it reads as your usual RIR once 3 sets of the lift are rated (0 only until then). Fixed, and the Guide's stall rule now matches the code (6 sessions or 3 weeks, at least 4 sessions).
  106. Plan: the Exercise library is a full-width button with its count; the ? sits at the top right on its own. The library's back button said Program; it says Plan.
  107. Today: This week reads This month in Month view (an earlier month by name, with its session count). The date line on the Start card is short enough for one line.
  108. Bug: content scrolled up showed through behind the clock (V's screenshots). A solid strip now covers the status bar area.
  109. Demo data looked at before setting up: whatever was changed while looking around goes back to the new-install defaults when the demo is cleared. Someone with a log of their own who loads the demo from Settings keeps their changes.
  110. V's report that dragging stopped working after showing or hiding sections: not reproduced. Sections shown or hidden in Settings, Layout or from the new line, then dragged by finger on Today, Stats and Settings, all moved and kept their order after a reload. It is now a test (tests/r28.js). If it happens again, the exact steps (which section, which tab, open or folded) will pin it down.
  111. Caught by the tests before release: putting the default order into a new install changed the order of keys in the synced settings, which would have made an r27 phone and an r28 phone rewrite each other's settings on every sync. Fixed (the key is added where older builds add it).
  112. "6 flagged" on day one (V): worse than reported, Full body 3 days showed 18 and Dumbbells 19. A muscle is now flagged only for what was logged, an injury or a priority; a plan lighter than the targets is a quiet note inside Muscles, and before any session the subtitle reads "after a few sessions". The Volume radar reads the plan until a full week is logged (a week in progress read as every region short), and says "target", not "band".
  113. From two independent reviews (one reading the code, one using the app as a new and an upgrading person), fixed with tests: clearing demo data could put back first-run settings over ones changed after starting for real, or from a stale snapshot after Erase everything or signing in; with auto-mark on, reps then RIR then the checkmark unticked the set (the first tap now confirms); the new menu icon and bottom link were near invisible in the light theme; Workout preview called green PR tags "flagged"; + Workout preview was offered on a rest day; Build my own routine landed with Days folded; Reset section order had no Undo (now in Layout, with Undo); a leftover Undo after Finish did nothing; "1 lifts"; Plan's ? sat on its own row; the routine picker's minutes now a range matching Today; jargon removed from the picker; Lifts opens on your most-logged lift; first-session recap says these are your starting numbers; spacing in Effort (RIR); a tap highlight in the Guide; "Program" in one tip.
- r27 (approved by V and published 2026-10-02 at https://ironapp.org; merged by Claude at V's go-ahead). V's requests of 2026-10-02:
  92. Report a problem and Sign in / Sign out one tap away: the round button at the top right of every tab is now a menu (account, sync state, Report a problem, Guide, Your data and backups). V chose this placement.
  93. Settings no longer repeats Report a problem or Help.
  94. Explanations: a Guide in the menu (Today, Logging, How targets are set, Volume and Stats, Plan and your own exercises, Sections, Your data), states the rules the numbers follow. Hidden press-and-hold explanations on setting labels became visible i buttons; dotted text shows its explanation on a tap; the RIR setting says what RIR is on screen (V's example). The estimate line in the workout preview reads "Est. 1RM 86.7 → 88.8 → 86.9 lb · flat" instead of ending in a stray arrow.
  95. Sections: every section on every tab checked to fold from its title, keep that across a re-render, move by a finger drag or the arrow keys, and keep its order after a reload (tests/r27.js).
  96. The numbers follow what you customise. An independent audit (a separate agent that had not seen the work) found 16 places where a custom routine, exercise or setting was ignored; 15 are fixed, each with a test (tests/custom.js): per-day rep ranges, grey reps after a new load, deload, warm-up and drop loads on the exercise's own step and not under the bar, the kg default step, changes reaching the session in progress, swaps getting a fitting plan, stalls expiring, a Movement (compound or isolation) choice for your own exercises, a range moved down, a 0 target, planned hard sets and the deload-week target, streaks after a routine switch, a load step of 0, Pick for me in a deload, warm-up order. The 16th is item 98. A second independent review of the finished r27 found more, all fixed with tests: a target taken from a months-old session when a lift returned to an old range, the bar floor wrongly applied to EZ-bar and fixed-bar curls and to per-side numbers, the exercise editor showing 2.3 kg, controls losing their spoken names next to an i, several Guide statements that did not match the app, and the 11-set flag overstating its source.
  97. Ready for the domain: the publish workflow keeps GitHub's domain file (pull request #7, merged 2026-10-02), and the install steps show the address the app is on.

It still does not set the next target: the target comes from the last full session, and when that is an earlier one, a tip on the target names its date.   ## Asked for on 2026-09-30, second round (built in r25, see Done)
  79. Guardrails: nothing logged or typed goes on a single tap. − Set takes an empty row first and asks before a filled one; removing an exercise with logged or typed sets asks; cardio entries (History and in a session), weigh-ins and tape entries ask. Undo still follows each.
  80. Continuity audit, beyond the swap: found and fixed (a) Short on time removed sets that were typed but not ticked, and whole exercises holding them; (b) a swap dropped typed, unticked sets; (c) Undo after Short on time did not put back the removed rows; (d) an exercise opened again folded when another exercise moved, was removed or a swap inserted one; (e) Discard did not ask when only a load was typed. Each has a test in `tests/r25.js` (section 14). Checked and fine: lighter and deload counts, date changes, units, settings changed mid-session, reload, cloud refresh (existing suites).
  81. The preview's yellow striped edges read as stray dotted lines: replaced by a labelled PREVIEW tag on the tab bar.
  82. Second copy pass (75): Pick for me's lead and cycle note, a lift's headline, the bodyweight trend line.

## Architecture history (moved from ARCHITECTURE.md in r56.2)

The detail of each finished phase of the move into typed modules, as ARCHITECTURE.md held it until r56.2: what moved, how it was proved, and what each review found. The design, the rules and the ratchet numbers stay in ARCHITECTURE.md.

### The comparisons with old builds retired or replaced (CL-3, 2.40)

V, 2026-11-09: comparisons that hold a new build to an old one and take each deliberate change out by hand are "poor
testing practices". In 2.40 each was retired or replaced by a check of the build under test against its rules. Every
file and section named here is in the repo as it was at 5b04fdc (branch r85) for anyone who needs to read or rerun it.

Retired whole, because by then they compared old builds only and read no code of the build under test:
- `tests/r36.js`: r35 and r52 on 2,000 seeded histories, proving the r36 split. Its one check on the build under test,
  that index.html is what src/ builds to (the only place GitHub's run checked it), moved to `tests/hygiene.js` (rule 8).
- `tests/r52.js`: r51 and r52 on the 1,000 wide histories, past midnight, with stale caches and on the demo (phase 3b).
- `tests/r53w.js`: r52 and r53 the same way, each difference held to the r53 rule that made it.
- `tests/r66b.js` section 6: Today's main card on r65.2 and r66 (held since by `tests/r67.js` section 4).
- `tests/wide.js`: the helpers only those used (staleRun, demoRun, r53Touches, honestTags and the rest).

Turned from a comparison of two old builds into a check of the build under test, every state they drew kept: each is
drawn with no page error and nothing undefined, NaN or [object Object] on screen (`screenLeaks`, `tests/h.js`):
- `tests/r58.js` section 1 (Plan, the library and Stats in 36 states; was r57 and r58),
- `tests/r59.js` section 1 (Today, the week bar and the calendar; was r58 and r59),
- `tests/r60.js` section 1 (the session screen; was r59 and r60),
- `tests/r61.js` section 1 (every tab and the session in three settings, and the Guide in each unit with RIR on and
  off; was r60 and r61, with the wide histories call for call),
- `tests/r62.js` section 1 (every sheet; was r61 and r62) and section 2 (Pick for me, the swaps, Limited equipment and
  Short on time on 1,000 wide histories: every call answers with no crash; was r61 and r62 call for call),
- `tests/r63.js` section 1 (every part r63 moved; was r62 and r63) and section 2 (import, the backup check and the demo
  on generated input: no crash; was r62 and r63 call for call).

Replaced, because they compared the build under test with an old one and carried hand-written exclusions:
- `tests/r57.js` section 4: History and Settings against r56.2 with eleven exclusions (heldOut, heldOutAccents,
  heldOutR66, heldOutR69, heldOutSched, heldOutR70, heldOutR73, heldOutR76, heldOutR79, heldOutR80, heldOutR83). Now:
  both screens in the same six states drawn with no page error and no leaked value, Settings showing the log's values
  and History its sessions newest first.

  workout, r66's name, r72's library lifts, 2.34's plates, 2.37's notes). Now normalize's own rules on the same logs:
  it never throws; read again it gives the same answer and drops nothing more; a log keeps every record and every set
  a build could have saved; an unknown field is kept and the record keeps its key order (keepExtra). Shown to fail
  when keepExtra keeps nothing.
- `tests/r63.js` section 4: the start against r62's with every later listener, hook name and action listed by hand.
  Now: drawing every tab four times round leaves the page with the same listeners (none piling up), and every action
  the source names by data-act is in the table.

Kept, as V's rulings name an old build's look: `tests/r70.js` section 5 (r69's compact rest pill, NEW-60), `tests/r66b.js`
section 5 (r65.2's full-width buttons, 368) and `tests/r33.js` part 1a2 (r32.1's At a glance). None carries an exclusion.

### The session rule's history (CL-2, 2.39)

In 2.39 V's one rule for every session was stated once (`setsHeldTo` and the comment above `idxShort` in usual.ts,
two lines in CLAUDE.md) and each case became a row of `tests/usual.js`. The comments it replaced carried the history
of how each case was found; they are kept here as they were.

The comment on `idxShort` (analytics.ts until CL-1):

```
/* Step 2b (r54, V's one rule for every session, PENDING 312 E: "a lift
   counts toward its next target only at its usual load and reps for its
   usual sets; fewer sets still count for its trend and PRs, and the target
   repeats", replacing the trimmed mark and r30's half rule; with V's
   answers of 2026-10-18 evening, a bad day falls short too, and a lift's
   usual sets are what it did in most of its last three sessions, never
   more than the plan). A lift's session not done lighter is short of its
   usual by its sets when it has fewer sets near its working load (setsAt)
   than its usual, and by its reading when, at a working load no heavier
   than the two sessions before it, its reading is more than USUAL_SHARE
   under its usual: the strength reading, reps alone, where it and those it
   is read with have one, else its reps a set at its working load, and that
   load, each over as many sets at the working load as the usual
   (readingOf), so a set beyond the usual never counts against it. Its
   usual is the middle of it and the two sessions of the lift before it in
   the range not done lighter (the higher of two with only one before it),
   so one session under it repeats the target and a second in a row is the
   usual, which the app then follows.
   On a lift the routine plans (`planSets`, the day it was done on first;
   null for a lift on no day of the routine, which reads its own sessions),
   its usual sets are never more than the plan's, so a lifter who does more
   sets than planned is not short when doing the plan. A session saved on a
   day of a routine keeps the sets its plan gave the lift then (`ps`, r58,
   339 h); once a session of the lift in the range since its last break did
   that many near its working load, those are the sets it must reach, so
   fewer sets than planned never become the usual (a 5 x 5 done as 4 x 5
   twice read as a 4-set lift, and its target asked for 4 sets the plan
   never set). Each session is read by its own plan, so a plan changed
   later never rereads the sessions before it (the r58 review: read by the
   plan as it is now, a plan raised from 3 sets to 4 marked every session of
   3 short and took the target back to the first of them). A session with
   none recorded (Freestyle, a lift on no day of the routine, one saved
   before r58, a blank session saved before r70) reads its own sets, as
   before. Since r70 a blank session records them too (NEW-51): Finish saves,
   on a lift the routine plans, the sets of its day nearest to what the
   session did, unless Today opens blank (Freestyle), so two one-set PR attempts in a
   row never make a 3-set lift a 1-set one, and, as on a routine's day, each
   session is read by what was saved with it, never by the plan or the mode
   as they are now (the review of r70, R70-1 and R70-2). On a lift labelled
   lighter or a deload but done at its usual load
   and reps (`tag`, step 2), the label cut the plan, so its usual sets are
   its own, and if it falls short it is not one of the sessions later ones
   are read against, so half the sets of a deload twice running never
   become the usual. A heavier working load than both before it is a step
   up, whose reps fall as a step costs them: never under its usual by its
   reading, as a trend reads it (r34.2, loadUp); a step too big is the
   engine's to read (sgBelowRange). The first session of a lift in a range,
   or back after a break (afterBreak), is its own usual. */
```

The comments on `nearestPlanSets` and `withPlanSets` (routines.ts):

```
/* The sets a routine plans for a lift on the day nearest to what a blank
   session did (NEW-51, r70): what the session is held to. A lift planned at
   one count is held to it. Planned at 5 sets one day and 3 another, a blank
   session of 1 or 3 sets is held to 3 and one of 5 to 5, a tie going to the
   fewer, so a blank session never asks more sets than the day it resembles
   (the review of r70, R70-1: held to the first day's 5, a session of 3 read
   short) and a 5-set session is judged on all five (R70-5: held to the
   fewest, 3, its weaker fourth and fifth sets were not read and the next
   target fell from 40 reps to 33). Only the days that plan the lift in the
   session's rep range count, all days when none does, since a session is
   read only against sessions of its own range (R70-6: 3 heavy sets of a lift
   planned 5 x 4-6 and 3 x 8-12 were held to the light day's 3, read in full,
   and the heavy day's load went up). */


/* A workout's exercises as Finish saves them, each lift on a day of a
   routine given the sets its plan gives it (planSetsOf), last, as normalize
   writes it. Since r70 a blank session (no day) records them too, while the
   routine is followed: each lift the routine in use plans is given the sets
   of the day nearest to its working sets, among the days that plan it in the
   session's rep range (nearestPlanSets), so two one-set
   sessions in a row never make it a 1-set lift (NEW-51); none in Freestyle, where Today
   opens blank and the lifter's own sets lead. Recorded once, at Finish, so a
   plan or the mode changed later never rereads the sessions before it (the
   r58 review, and R70-1 and R70-2 in r70's). None on an edit, which keeps the
   sets saved with the session (Finish carries them over with the fields it
   does not rebuild, keepExtra). */
```

CLAUDE.md's sentence before 2.39: a lift sets its next target only at its usual load and reps for its usual sets (in a
session on a day of the routine, the sets its plan gave it that day, saved with it, once a session did them: r58; in a
blank session, the sets of the routine's day nearest to what it did among the days that plan it in its rep range,
saved with it the same way, none in Freestyle: r70, NEW-51, R70-6).

### Phase 4 in detail (r56 and r56.1)

**Two releases, so a fault across devices has one suspect.** r56 moves storage and sync out of the legacy script into typed modules and changes nothing they do: the same storage keys, the same sync documents byte for byte, key order included, the same merge. r56.1 then makes the sync changes V placed there (PENDING 328 a to c, 305, 307, 308) on the typed code, each with a test that fails on r56.

**What moved in r56 (built 2026-10-20 and 21).** `src/12-storage.js` and `src/13-sync.js` whole, `cloudStop` and the three listeners from `src/14-account.js` (another tab's save, the page hidden or left), `snapshot` and `capturePost` from `src/18-ui.js`, the settings kept aside for the demo from `src/26-import.js` (`DEMOSET_KEY`, `demoFirstRun`, `demoKeep`, `demoRestore`), and from `src/11-data.js` the state's own data (`freshState`, `starterRoutine`, `starterSigs`, `SEC_TABS`, `HIDEABLE`, `HIDEABLE_KEYS`, `HIDE_TABS`, `MAIN_FIXED`) and from `src/25-helpers.js` `tplSigs` and `plainTemplate`, which the sync reads. The display helpers that sat in the storage part (`distU`, `distShow`, `distIn`) moved to the legacy helpers part, for the screens (phase 5); `setSaveState`, which draws the menu button, moved to the screen helpers part, and `syncLine` (the sync line redrawn in place, the snippet `setCloud` and `acctRerender` shared) is named there. The account flow and its sheets stay for phase 5.
- `normalize.ts` (layer 3): `keepExtra`, `cleanCardio`, `cleanSess`, `normalize` (with `normalize.dropped` and `keepExtra.n`, which the suites read, kept as properties of the functions), `freshState`, `starterRoutine`, `starterSigs`, `tplSigs`, `plainTemplate`. Raw input is read through three readers (`rec`, an object or array read by name; `arr`, a list; `parseJson`, JSON text as an unknown value) in `records.ts`, shared with the sync, and one of its own (`spread`, an object's fields spread as `{...v}` spreads any value), so the casts an unknown value needs are in those readers alone; the one `Record<string, unknown>` alias (`Rec`) is the open type they read.
- `storage.ts` (layer 3): the keys built from `KEY` (`ENV`, `KEY`, `LOGK`, `WKVIEW_KEY`, `DEMOSET_KEY`; `SYNC_KEY` is sync.ts's, and the account's keys stay with the account flow), where the save stands (`device`: `ok`, `boot`, `hadLocal`, `hadLog` and `lastSaved`, the five flags the legacy script kept as `storageOk`, `storageBoot`, `bootHadLocal`, `HAD_LOG` and `lastSaved`: an import cannot be assigned to, so they became one object, as `state` became `setState` in 3a), the error log (`logBuf`, `logCut`, `log`), `storageTest` and `load`, Undo's steps and their IndexedDB copy (`undoStack`, `snapshot`, `capturePost`, `undoPersist`, `undoRestore`, `undoForget`), the demo's settings aside, and `backupDue` and `saveStateNow`. `storageStart()` runs what the part ran as the page loaded.
- `sync.ts` (layer 3): every write of the log (`saveNow`, `saveSoon`, `commit`: a save goes to this device at once and to the account after it, so the write path is one module and neither module imports the other), everything in the sync part, the sync notes' load and save, `cloudStop`, and the three listeners; `syncStart()` runs what the parts ran as the page loaded. `providers.ts`, the services the page runs on (the account, the reports, Claude's db where the suites inject their stand-in, and the backend's `ironlogCloud`), is now layer 3, since the sync reads it: a change to the layer list, with the `window` declarations of those services gathered there.
- The screens are called back through one object of effects in `store.ts` (`fx`: `render`, `rerenderKeepScroll`, `toast`, `setSaveState`, `syncLine`, `obGate`), which the legacy script fills once at start (`setFx`, the first statement of the legacy script, before `storageStart()` and `syncStart()`), so layer 3 imports nothing from the screens. `cloud` is typed field by field (`CloudDb`, `SyncNote`, `Base` in `store.ts`), its index signature gone; `State` gained `version`, every setting as `normalize()` keeps it (`Settings` in `types.ts`), the tape entries (`Measurement`) and the session in progress (`DraftLike`).
- `layout.ts` moved to layer 1 (it imports nothing, and `normalize` reads its section defaults), a change to the layer list and nothing else, as `format.ts` and `charts.ts` moved in 3b.
- What ran at the top of those parts when the page loaded (the preview's mark on the page, whether a log was here, the error log, the error listeners, the save read into the store, the sync notes, the device id, this device's hashes, the listeners) runs from the legacy script's start, in the same order, through `storageStart()` and `syncStart()`; no module runs anything on import (`ENV` and `KEY` are values read from the build's flag, as `THREE_URLS` is), so `tests/engine.js` still loads every module in Node.
- Three things the typed code does differently, each on data no build writes, and said here so no one hunts for them: a list field in a document that is not a list reads as empty (`merge3List`, `unionById`, `applyChunk`, `assemble`), where the sync used to stop on it with a `TypeError` and retry for ever; a record in a document that is not a record is read as an empty one (`normChunk`), which `normalize()` cleaned to the same document before; and a cleaner called on one entry with no position (`cleanCardio` on a typed cardio entry, which always has an id) gives `did` no position, as before. `tests/r56.js` proves the rest unchanged on 486 raw logs, half of them damaged by hand.

**How r56 is proved to change nothing.** Every suite as it is, in Chromium and WebKit, the storage and sync ones above all (`migration`, `seams`, `integrity`, `dataflow`, `r22`, `accounts`, `r55b`, `r55-1b`, `preview`, `fuzz`); and `tests/r56.js`, new: `normalize`, `chunksOf`, `assemble`, `baseOf` and `mergeChunk` on the page of r56 and on `baselines/r55.html` give the same JSON, key order included, and the same `normalize.dropped` and `keepExtra.n`, on 240 wide histories (tests/wide.js), each again damaged by a seeded hand (odd values in every kind of field, unknown fields, records of the wrong shape, duplicate ids, a broken session in progress, trash of every kind), and hand-made edge cases (a cardio kind that is a list, a key Object already has, a settings object that is a string, bands the wrong way round, a routine with no days). It passed before the move as after. One independent review.

**r56.1, on the typed code, each change with a test that fails on r56** (`tests/r56-1.js` on r56's own page, `baselines/r56.html`; the account question in `tests/r56-1b.js` against the stand-in server, through the real account service). The design, written before the code (V, 2026-10-20), then built on 2026-10-20 and 21, with what the building found written into each item:

1. **One workout open on two phones (328 a and c).** The session in progress is one document, merged whole: of two copies edited since they last agreed, the later edit replaced the other, and the loser's ticked sets were gone on every phone. Now its merge base keeps the content, not only a fingerprint: the draft's sync note holds the copy both sides last agreed on (`Base.draft`, beside `d`; a note an older build saved has none, and merges as before until the next agreement). `mergeChunk('draft', ...)` then merges two copies of the same workout (the same session id on both sides and in the base) exercise by exercise and set by set, as Undo's `mergeDraft` merges by exercise: exercises keyed by their own ids (`bid`), or, on one an older build made without one, by exercise id and occurrence (`exId#n`), since keyed by occurrence the same lift twice, the first removed on one phone while the other ticked the second, came out twice (PENDING 333, F9); a set judged by its row (the rows both phones started from are the base's; a row added on one side alone is kept after the row it follows on that side, so a warm-up added at the top stays at the top (it went to the bottom, PENDING 333, F10); a row removed on one side alone goes, unless the other side changed it, since keeping data is the recoverable mistake; rows are matched by their own ids, `rid`, which every row gets since r56.1, and a row a build before it made, which has none, by its place among such rows in a workout in progress, where every row stays in place ticked or not, and by what it holds in a join, since a saved session keeps no unticked rows and a place means nothing there: matched by place, a set ticked on one phone took the place of the one finished on the other and was lost on both, PENDING 333, `tests/r56-1.js` (1b); matched by what it holds, a row is matched to the agreed copy's rows, or, with no agreed copy (a note from a build before r56.1), to the other side's rows one to one, since matched to nothing every set both phones had was saved twice, F5; places hold while rows are added at the end, and a warm-up an older build adds at the top moves every place, which is why every device is updated before two are used on one workout, F8 in PENDING); a set changed on one side alone takes that side, changed on both takes the later edit (`remoteWins`) and counts a conflict; a block's own fields (its note, its adjustment, its marks) and the workout's (its notes, day name, marks, check-in, cardio rows) are merged value by value against the base (`merge3Val`). Two copies of different workouts (one phone finished or discarded and started another, or two started apart) still take the later edit whole, as before, and the copy that loses is never dropped: its ticked sets go into the log as a session, with its cardio rows and check-in, exactly as a first link keeps another phone's workout (`draftSession`, `draftCardio`, r55), and the toast says so ("...replaced by the one on another device. Its ticked sets are saved in History."). A workout closed on one phone (finished or discarded: the account's draft document empty, or this phone's) while the other ticked on stays closed, whichever edit was later, since closing is the lifter's call; the sets ticked since are kept, and the cardio rows typed since with them (the join kept the sets alone, and a copy with cardio and no ticked set was dropped whole, PENDING 333, F6): when the session of the same id is in the log (finished there), they join it, merged set by set against the copy both agreed on (`Base.draft`, turned into a session as Finish would, `mergeSession`), the saved session keeping its order and the sets done here since added after it (a build before r56.1 saves no row ids, so its rows are matched by what they hold; `tests/mixed.js`), the session's own fields (its duration, its notes, its plan) taken from the finished copy where this phone did not change them, and with no agreed copy (a note from a build before r56.1) the saved session's values standing wherever the two differ; a flush reads that session's half-month document first (`sessionFromCloud`, `chunkOf` of the workout's date, taken in as a pull takes it) whenever this phone's copy lost, so the session is there to join and never made twice; when there is none (discarded on the other phone) the ticked sets are saved as a session of their own, as above. An edit of a saved session in progress (`editingId`) is one record, as now: the saved session it edits is already kept. Where it bites: `keepLost`, called after the draft document is taken in by a flush and by a pull, for this phone's copy when it lost and for the account's copy when a later edit here replaced it; a copy that lost nothing (the same workout, still open), an edit, or one with nothing ticked is left alone. The join or the save is an edit made here now (`cloud.editAt` of the session's document), and the flush that follows carries it. What was done is said in the toast: "The workout in progress was saved on another device. What was done here since was added to it." (joined), "...was closed on another device. Its ticked sets are saved in History." (saved, nothing open), "...was replaced by the one on another device. Its ticked sets are saved in History." (saved, another workout open), "...was replaced by the one on another device. Its ticked sets are in History." (the session of its id is in the log, finished there or kept there from this copy); and for the account's copy kept here, "Sets ticked on another device were saved in History." or "...were added to the session saved here." The agreed copy in the note is a copy of its own (a JSON round trip in `baseOf`): `normalize()` rebuilds a workout in place and the logger edits the workout in progress in place, and the first build of this held the live workout in the note, so the base moved with every tick and the merge took the account's side each time; `tests/r56-1.js` found it at its first check. The first tests: the audit's reproduction (327) and the review's (328 a: a first link keeps the new phone's workout, the account's phone ticks a set before its next pull; 328 c: a set ticked while signed out on a workout another phone also ticked).

2. **A phone holding another account's log (328 b, V's call of 2026-10-20).** A phone signed out of one account and signed in to another merged the first account's log into the second as a first link. Now `cloudInit` reads the notes before it would clear them: the notes belong to another account (`cloud.acct` set and not this account) and the phone holds a log (`realCount`, weigh-ins, tape entries, cardio, a workout in progress, a custom exercise or a routine that is the lifter's own, as the first-link test reads it); then nothing syncs until the lifter answers one sheet (the confirm sheet, which already offers a third choice, through `fx.ask`, which gives the modules a question with two answers and Cancel; `confirmBox` and `closeModal` learn an `onClose`, so Cancel reaches the module, each an edit of a line the legacy script has): "A log from another account is on this phone. Add it to this account, or use this account's log?", with Add it, Use this account's log, and Cancel. Add it: with nothing kept aside for this account, the notes are cleared and the sync starts as a first link, the merge as it is.
