Use this account's log: the phone's log is kept aside first, whole, for the account it belongs to, with the notes that go with it (`keepAside`, `asideOf`, `dropAside` in `storage.ts`), so what only this phone holds is not lost; it is kept in IndexedDB (`ironlog-aside`, the account as the key), as Undo's copies are, since that room is separate from localStorage's and far larger: kept in localStorage at first, a log of 40% of the room kept aside made every save of a log of 62% of it fail (PENDING 333, `tests/room.js`); the copy is taken as the log is when it is replaced (written again if a set was ticked while it was written, the account's own copy read before, so nothing waits between the two), and a copy taken back into use is removed only once the log it became is saved on the phone: the next sign-in to that account on this phone finds it and starts from it, with the notes it kept, so it syncs as a continuation (the account's deletions honoured, as any sync does), the lifter's log of that day going aside in its turn; a log kept aside for the account signed in to is the base when the answer is Add it: the log here is joined to it, its records winning a clash, under its own notes (PENDING 333, F4); Your data lists a log kept aside (its account, its sessions) until then, through a line a module writes into the existing template (`asideLine`), and Erase everything clears it with the rest of the device; then Undo is cleared (a step held the other account's log, and undoing it would sync that log into this account), the notes are cleared, and the sync starts on a fresh state, which adopts the account's copy. Cancel: the notes stay as they were, the sync stays off, and the sign-out goes through the account service alone (`AUTH().signOut()`), never `cloudStop(true)`; a sheet opened over the question, or a crash's Try again, closes it as Cancel (`openModal` and `crashRetry` read an unanswered confirm as `closeModal` does), so the sync waiting on the answer never waits for ever. A phone with no room to keep the log aside (`keepAside` fails, the quota) replaces nothing: it says so ("There is not enough room on this phone to keep the other log aside. Export a backup of it first.") and the sign-in is undone as Cancel undoes it. While the question is open every other start of a sync is held (`cloudInit` returns at once, `acctRetry` too). An account the notes name that no longer exists is guarded the same way (the notes name it, the sheet still asks; Add it is the only safe answer the lifter needs, Use this account's log keeps the log aside under that id). A phone whose notes were forgotten (Sign out, a restore, Copy my log in, demo data, Erase everything) has no account to ask about and is not asked, as now; a log kept aside for the account signed in to comes back all the same, the log here joined to it (PENDING 333, F3). The suites: the review's reproduction (a deletion made in the second account reaching the first), each answer, Cancel's state, a sync started while the question is open, a sheet opened over it, no room for the copy, the aside log taken back with its notes, and `tests/r56-1b.js` the same through the real account service.

   **After the stack's review (PENDING 333; `tests/r56-1c.js`, and F2 and F3 in `tests/r56-1b.js`).** The review found four ways the question and the log kept aside lost or leaked a log, and they are now one rule (`takeAccount` in `sync.ts`), settled before any sync: (a) the log kept aside for the account signed in to comes back with its own notes on every path (taken back without them on Add it, or not asked, it synced as a first link, undoing edits and bringing back deletions on every phone, F4), and it is looked for whenever the notes do not name this account, also when they name none (after the menu's Sign out, which forgets them, it stayed aside for good, F3); (b) nothing is left half done: the log is replaced and saved, then the notes naming this account, and a save the phone refuses puts both back as they were and undoes the sign-in, with "This phone could not save this account's log, so nothing was changed." (the notes were saved regardless, so the next start synced the other account's log under these notes, deleting this account's sessions on every phone, F1); (c) one tab at a time: every tab hears a sign-in through the account service, so each asked and acted on the notes it read before, and a second tab's answer kept aside, under the first account, the log the first tab had put in use (F2); the rule now runs under a Web Lock (`oneTab`), a tab that waited reads the notes and the log as the other left them (`readStored`) and finds the switch made, another tab's notes carry their account to this tab (`onStorage`), and Cancel's sign-out is waited for, so a tab waiting its turn finds it signed out. Undo is cleared whenever the log becomes another account's (Use this account's log, or a log taken back from aside). A browser without Web Locks (Safari before 15.4) runs the rule at once, as before. Not tested: an edit made in a second tab in the same instant another tab replaces the log; by reading, the other-tab listener merges it into the new log, as it merges any edit made there.

   **After the review of that fix (PENDING 333; `tests/r56-1c.js` G2 to G10, the last case of `tests/r56-1b.js`).** (d) The notes on the phone describe the log on the phone: `saveMeta` writes nothing while the log's own save is failing (a note saved ahead of the log made the next start read the log as edited back and delete a synced workout everywhere), and nothing over newer notes from another tab. A tab reads another's write only once it arrives, so a second tab hearing the menu's Sign out a millisecond late wrote the old notes back and the first tab took them; the notes now carry `ep`, the moment they last stopped describing the log before them (`cloudStop(true)`, an account switch, a first link), `saveMeta` does not write over notes with a later `ep`, a tab that hears notes with an earlier one writes its own back (`onStorage`), and `ep` is absent until first set, so a build before it reads and writes the notes as before. Notes that name no account sign in as a first link, their documents' notes dropped, since they cannot be a continuation of any account. (e) A log kept aside comes back as it was when the log here holds nothing of the lifter's (`holdsLog`, read before anything changes); otherwise only what is theirs is joined to it, never demo data or a ready-made routine as it came. Of two workouts in progress the one on this phone stays open, as a first link keeps this device's, and the other's ticked sets and cardio are saved in History, with "The workout in progress on this phone stays open. The one kept for this account is saved in History." (f) In the merges: an agreed copy with nothing ticked is an empty base, not a missing one; with truly no agreed copy (a note from r56 or before) rows are matched by content one to one, so two equal sets ticked on two phones are one, as the same row is; cardio of a workout lost to the other phone's merges three ways against the agreed copy (`keepLost`), so an edit stands and a deletion stays; a join keeps the order of the session saved on the other phone; and a mid-workout adjustment carries its row's id (`adj.rid`, absent on adjustments made before), so after a reload or a merge it stays on the rows below its set.

3. **Every stored value through one typed check (305).** The sync notes are read entry by entry (`SyncNote` and `Base` checked field by field, a note that fails dropped with a line in the error log, so a damaged note never reads as agreement), the error log's entries too (`LogEntry`), the settings kept aside for the demo through `normalize()` (as now), and the cloud documents' fields (`json` a string, `at` and `et` numbers) as now. The casts in `notesIn` go, and `tests/hygiene.js` fails on `localStorage`, `indexedDB` or `JSON.parse` named anywhere in `src/app/` but `storage.ts` and `sync.ts` (the legacy parts keep their account and screen keys until phase 5). A cardio entry's kind and time are checked against the lists, not looked up (`CARDIOL[String(kind)]` let any key Object has, and a list, through).

4. **The sync's status as one set of states (307).** `cloud.status` is `'local' | 'connecting' | 'saving' | 'synced' | 'offline'`, moved only by `setCloud`; a string from anywhere else is refused by the type checker.

5. **A start time a Date can hold (308).** `normalize()` keeps a session's `t` and a workout's `startedAt` only when `new Date(t)` is a date (an integer, 0 < t <= 8,640,000,000,000,000), so a damaged save can no longer show "Invalid Date" in History; one that cannot is dropped, as any damaged field is.

Each of these changes what the typed code does, so `tests/r56.js`'s comparison with r55 is pinned to the move (r56's page kept as `baselines/r56.html`, refreshed from the published r56 if that build changes before it is merged) and r56.1's own suites hold the new behaviour: each case fails on r56's page.

**Progress (kept current, so the work survives a restart).** r56: started 2026-10-20, branch `r56` on `r55.1`; the move built 2026-10-21 (`normalize.ts`, `storage.ts`, `sync.ts`; the legacy script down to 424 declarations and 6,366 lines), `tests/r56.js` and every suite green in Chromium (`tests/r43.js`'s simulated next build now changes `normalize.ts`, through `scripts/join.js`'s `build({ override })`, which takes a module too); then the review. r56.1: built 2026-10-20 and 21 on branch `r56.1` on `r56`, the five items above, `tests/r56-1.js` and `tests/r56-1b.js` green and failing on r56's page; then the whole run and the review.

### r53 in detail (a behaviour release between phases)

**Why it is not a phase.** V's rulings of 2026-10-18 change what a lifter sees: a day's label must not override what a lift did ("tied at exercise level"), a PR is a PR whenever it happens, and Finish's offer adds an exercise at its normal-day sets. The audit V asked for the same day added two: no stall while a flagged injury holds a lift's load, and the coach holding a deload back only after a lighter week, as its rule says. Phase 4 must change nothing a lifter sees, so these go first, alone.

**How the code changed.** Seven moved declarations changed through `edits` (A change to the legacy script between phases, above): `idxPRsIn` (PRs on every day), `IDXIn` (one step added, and the PR list passed to the stall test), `idxAutoLighter` (its usual-load test read through the shared `atUsualLoad`, the same comparison), `idxLiftIn` (the injury, and a PR on a session left out of the stall's stretch since the third-last session of it counted as movement, on a lift with a reading only one Recent PRs lists, read from the PR list it is now given), `coachIn` (its stall tip's words), `recentLighterIn` (a lighter week read from what the lifts did: more than half its sessions with half or more of their lifts done lighter, once it has ended or from the second, or, a deload begun mid-week, its last two or more sessions deloads done lighter, tagged deload or in the marked week; rest weeks; a marked deload week with nothing trained in it) and `planForIn` (a lift on no day of the routine planned from its own last full session). Four were added with their exact code: `USUAL_SHARE`, `atUsualLoad`, `atUsualLevel` (as many sets as each of the sessions it is compared with first, then the load and the reading, or the reps where there is no reading) and `idxTaggedFull`. Seven legacy declarations moved: `routineUpdates` to `routines.ts` as `routineUpdatesIn`, to change (`moved: true`, its new code stated), with its adapter; `whyTarget` to `session.ts`, to change its note on what does not set a target (a session where the lift was trimmed or done lighter than usual, or on a lighter day, in a deload or on an exercise made lighter had fewer reps or sets than usual, no longer lighter and deload sessions as a whole); the Guide (`guideSheet`) to `guide.ts`, its text changes listed as pairs; and `AUTH`, `FB`, `rirScale` and `halfLbl`, which the Guide reads, to `providers.ts` and `effort.ts` unchanged. Nothing in the legacy script was edited in place; it lost 7 declarations and 149 lines. `scripts/depmap.js` now reads a module export that reads the store, the live model or the page as shared state for the legacy code calling it (`moduleReach`): with live.ts as the rule, the seven legacy declarations that call `rirScale` counted as pure. `tests/moved.js` gained a check that an edit takes away no name it does not state, took Function, Reflect, Proxy and WebAssembly off the globals new code may read, and renames from the code as esbuild reprints it: the minifier picks short names by how often each character occurs, so the compiler's pure-call comments, or an arrow written as an escape, gave the same code two sets of names.

**How it is proved.** `tests/r53.js` drives the app's own flows on the starter routine, and each rule fails on r52. Three parts also fail on this release's own earlier versions, found by its checks and its independent review: a lift with no strength reading (a hanging leg raise, a plank, pull-ups before any weigh-in) read at its usual load whatever its reps, so a lighter day of 8 leg raises after 12s counted in full and the next target fell from 25+ reps to 17+; half the sets at the usual load counted in full, so two sets of 10 after four added load off half the work; and the lighter-week test, counting only weeks where every session was tagged, asked for a deload the week after one begun mid-week. The second review found four more, each now failing on the version before it: half the sets still counted in full when one earlier session had stopped at half (the sets are now held to each earlier session's, not the fewest); a deload week marked on a Saturday after a normal week held the coach back for four weeks with no deload done; deload sessions done at full for every lift held it back too; and two Lighter days early in a week did not. The third found the coach treating one deload session late in a normal week as a deload week, where one Lighter day was not: the lighter week is now read the same way for both, from what the lifts did. It also found that clearing an injury lets the held lift read stalled until its next session, since the held sessions are not recorded (PENDING 311, which needs stored data and a migration). The fourth, of the changes after the third, found the comparison with r35 refusing the stall tip's new words (now read in r53's words by `coachSame`), the target note saying fewer sets on any day where r30 keeps half the sets a full session on a normal day (now said for lighter days and deloads only), the Guide using "lighter week" for two rules (each now stated), and a four-day lifter who taps Deload week on Wednesday and deloads Thursday and Friday being asked for a deload again (a week whose last two or more sessions were lighter now counts). The fifth, of the changes after the fourth, found that rule hanging on the weekday: two Lighter days on Thursday and Friday after three normal days held the deload back where the same two on Monday and Tuesday did not, and a heavy and light programme with its light days late in the week never had one suggested (only deloads, the sessions the Deload week button makes, now make that run); the target note and the stall tip silent on a lighter day with fewer reps and on Make it lighter (both now named); the Guide still calling the weekly averages' week a lighter week (now said as what it is); and a lift read stalled the day after a PR on a Lighter day, beside it in Recent PRs, since the stall test left the session out and its PR with it (r52 did the same after a trimmed session; a PR on such a session since the third-last of the stretch now counts as movement). Each fails on the version before it, and the half-of-lifts boundary and the run's reset each fail `tests/moved.js` when changed; `tests/coherence.js` now reads Stalls against Recent PRs on screen, and fails on r52, whose trimmed session's PR sat beside its lift listed as stalled. The sixth found that rule ending a stall on a PR no screen shows (90 x 9 on a Lighter day after 100 x 8, a rep PR at the lighter load), which took a deload the lifts needed away again (now only a PR Recent PRs lists counts, on a lift with a reading); the run of deloads missing a Freestyle lifter's deload begun mid-week, since a blank session in a marked week is not tagged (now any session in the marked week counts as a deload session); the stall tip and the Guide promising no PR on any day where the stall reads one rep range (now said for the range); and the comparison with r35 leaving 28 histories out whole (now compared with the same allowance as against r52). Each new case fails on the version before it, and each condition, the range among them, fails `tests/moved.js` when broken. `tests/r53w.js` runs r52 as published and this build on `tests/r52.js`'s 1,000 wide histories, each tagged lift first made lighter than its usual (`honestTags`: a generator draws loads on their own, and a tagged lift at its usual level now counts in full, which reaches everything read from the log). Every call with the same arguments gives r52's answer unless a rule changes it, and then only as that rule says, and on every history compared this build's PRs are r52's on the same log with every deload and lighter label taken off: IDX but for the PRs of lighter and deload days and, with an injury flagged, the stall of each lift it loads and the muscle scores those stalls set, and likewise a lift this build reads as not stalled with a PR on a session left out of its stall since the third-last of the stretch (`prOff`); the stalled lifts, and the lifts moving up, but for those; a lift's own plan, its sets checked against its last full session and its range against that session's where Your training sets none; the lighter-week test, each build's answer checked against its own rule read from the log; the coach, a deload suggested or held back by that test, a stalled lift read at its own range, or such a lift's stall no longer named; Finish's offer, identical on a normal day and on a lighter day or in a deload but for its added sets, checked against the day's plan read from the routine and the log. A call given a lift's own plan is asked again in r52 with this build's arguments and must give this build's answer. A history with a tagged lift still at its usual level is counted and left to `tests/r53.js`; since every tagged lift is made lighter first, how a lift counted in full reaches the other readers is held by `tests/r53.js` and `tests/moved.js`'s checks, not here. Then the scenarios `tests/r52.js` runs: a page left open past midnight, each cache read stale, and the demo, with the same allowances. `tests/r36.js` still compares r35 on its 2,000 histories with the same readings (`honestTags`, `coachSame`): it takes this build's PRs on lighter and deload days out and compares the coach as r53 changes it, and it counts and leaves out a history where a tagged lift counts in full (2 of 2,000) and, where a PR on a day left out of a stall lifts one r35 read, compares that stall, the muscle scores it set and the coach as against r52 (`freed`, `idxSame`, `coachOk`, shared in `tests/wide.js`). `tests/r52.js` is pinned to r51 and `baselines/r52.html`, so the 3b move stays proved as it was; its scenarios are shared with `tests/r53w.js` through `tests/wide.js`. `tests/engine.js` calls the 75 adapters and, since r53, `avgWeeksTxt`, whose lines no adapter reached.

**What r53 leaves, said now.** A lifter on a lighter day sees a PR at Finish, not as the set is logged (`refreshPR`, legacy, with its toast and helpers, phase 5; PENDING 311). A lift on no day of the routine takes its sets from its last full session even when the analytics read that session as lighter from its loads (an untagged deload): `planForIn` is in layer 1 and the lighter reading in layer 2 (PENDING 313). A session promoted by step 2b is not one of the sessions step 2 compares later ones with, since step 2 runs first, and `planForIn` reads the day's label, not what the lift did (313). The weekly volume average reads a lighter week from its labels or from half or more of its lifts read lighter (r30), Stats' last lighter week line (`lastLighterWeek`, legacy) a third way, and the coach from what the lifts did (312). Clearing an injury lets a held lift read stalled until its next session (311). Its reps in reserve stay the plan's or the profile's, on purpose: last time's as the target would hide a load that was too light.

**Progress:** built; checked against r52 and r35 as above; reviewed six times independently, every blocker fixed with a check that fails on the version before, the rest fixed or recorded (PENDING 311 to 314); not yet published.

### r54 in detail (a behaviour release between phases)

**Why it is not a phase.** The whole-app audit V asked for on 2026-10-18 (a simulated lifter on the real page, what a lifter expects written down first) found 11 problems, every one already in r52 (PENDING 315). V chose to ship the reviewed r53 first and to fix them in r54, under the way of working approved the same day (PENDING 316): legacy code fixed in place with a failing test first, and no token-for-token move check.

**How the code changed.** In the modules, first the audit's fixes (315): `engine.ts` (`rackStepIn` reads the load in hand when nothing is logged; `snapUnitIn` takes a load as typed only when exact in the unit on screen (`onStep`, `TYPED_STEP`), puts one off the step on a step (the step below on lighter days, else the nearest, never under `loadFloorIn` or, but on lighter days, a tenth under the load done, `LIGHTER_LOAD`) and names the step the shown load took; `rackGridIn` reads lb dumbbells on a 5 lb rack (317 g); `sgEasyIn` and `easierTextIn` word effort in the lifter's scale; `heavierLoadIn`, moved from the legacy script), `format.ts` (`effScale`, `effAt`), `effort.ts` (`effShow`, moved), `analytics.ts` (`lighterTally`, and `idxLightWeeks` on it; `stallStretch` and `stallWeeksIn`), `weeks.ts` (`avgFirstWeekIn`, which the averages start from; `avgWaitIn`, the one reason no average shows yet; `cycleMixIn` and `muscleStatusIn`, moved, a muscle on plan as a status of its own; `recentLighterIn` counting a week with nothing trained; `lastLighterWeekIn`, moved and rewritten to the averages' reading; `weeksAvgIn`, the radar's fallback weeks; the words `LIGHTER_WEEK_WORDS`, `LIGHTER_WEEK_TIP`, `FIRST_WEEK_REASON`, `FIRST_WEEK_WHY` and `ON_PLAN_TIP`), `routines.ts` (`plannedPerWeek` and `dayShare`, a cycle's whole sessions a week, 317 d; `plannedSetsIn`, moved; `isTrainDay`, `nextTrainIdx`, `dayCreditIn`), `profile.ts` (`defBandIn` and `appBandIn`, moved, `fitFloorsIn`, and `refitIn`, which moves targets fitted by the old reading once at start), `coach.ts` (`STALL_WHY`, the stall's span, "do every planned set" when the plan reaches the target, the lines with no average yet) and `guide.ts`. Then V's answers (317) and what r53 left open (311 to 314): `analytics.ts` (`idxShort`, V's one rule, marking a lift's session short of its usual by its sets or by its reading; `idxAutoLighter` restarting at a break and keeping a labelled lift done in full as `tag`, with `atUsualWork` and the working load in `atUsualLoad` in place of `atUsualLevel` and `idxTaggedFull`; `stallScopesIn`, every range in use, with held sessions left out of the stall test; `sessionWord` and `sessionWordsIn`; `idxPRsIn` judging each set at the load it stands for after a change of units, and `prFrameIn` giving the live check and the toast the same), `formulas.ts` (`lighter` and `offTarget` in place of `tagged` and `offSession`; `afterBreak`; `typedUnit` and `prSameLoad`), `trend.ts` (a trend read from the last break), `engine.ts` (`backDaysIn`; the bad-day and below-range cases reading the sessions that set targets; no help shown on an assisted lift's first session), `plans.ts` (new, layer 2: `planForIn` and `routineUpdatesIn`, moved from `routines.ts`, which keeps `basePlanIn` and `routinePlanIn`), `units.ts` (`DEFAULT_STEP`, `TYPED_STEP` and `onStep`, read where the engine, `loads.ts` and `gym.ts` typed the numbers again), `library.ts` (`LOAD_STEPS`, the dumbbell rack's spacings), `prs.ts` (`BAND_SHORT`, the same words as `BAND_WORDS`, merged into it), `loads.ts` (`emptyLoad`), `sheets.ts` (new, layer 4: `morphInto`; its `followViewport`, which sized the sheets by the part of the screen that shows, was taken out after V's look at the preview, PENDING 322), `records.ts` (`KNOWN_SEX`) and the record and model types (an exercise's `inj`, a block's `full`; an exposure's `held`, `short` and `tag`). Then the independent review's findings (on commit 2478d73): `analytics.ts` (`setsAt` and `usualSets`, a session's sets near its working load and at its usual load; `readingOf` and `repsOf`, a session read against its usual over as many sets as the usual; `stallReads`, the sessions the stall test and its span read; `stallWordsIn`, the stall's span in the one wording the coach and `exHeadline` say); and, found after it, `coach.ts`'s `atRangeTopIn`, the one test of a stalled lift at the top of its range for the coach and `exHeadline`, read at the plan of the day its last session was done on. Sixteen adapters added, 91 in all. In the legacy script, fixed in place: `exHeadline` (the stall's span and its tip), the adherence line, `regionSrc` and `regionBody`, the Volume help, Stats' line on the last lighter week, `pastDraft` (never a deload), `ACT.obOwn` (rest days), History's rows (a deload named once, each lift's reading), Finish (no trimmed mark, the hold kept, the help asked for, the question's words), Short on time (no mark), `startFree` (a deload in a deload week), `applyPick` and Pick for me (the full count), `goalState` (every set), `refreshPR` (any day), `livePR` and `prBeat` (on `prFrame`), an empty load field's grey text, `renderModal` (the account sheet in place), `closeModal` (a turn stopped), and the words that read the trimmed mark. The style sheet places the sheets as r53 did. The legacy script lost 7 declarations and 48 lines (511 to 504, 7,436 to 7,388).

**How it is proved.** `tests/r54.js` (the audit's fixes) and `tests/r54b.js` (the one rule, breaks, the plan a lift is done at, 311 to 314 and the change of units) drive the app's flows, and each of their cases fails on r53 (`node tests/r54.js baselines/r53.html`, likewise r54b) unless it says it guards; `tests/coherence.js` gained a lighter-week case that fails on r53; `tests/r39.js` a sheet closed mid-turn, which fails on r53 (the suite reads a page argument since r54). The independent review's cases in `tests/r54b.js` fail on the build it read (`node tests/r54b.js` with that build's page) but the back-off set, which guards it. Expectations in older suites changed with r54's rules on purpose, each with the reason beside it: `tests/r29.js` (a log's first week out of the averages, twice), `tests/r30.js` (the Stats line's words; a log one week longer, so the week reached for is not the first), `tests/r31.js` (the radar's two notes; a squat's back-off fives read against its usual, after 220 x 5 x 5 setting the target and after 330 x 5 x 5 repeating it), `tests/r32.js` (a bad day under its usual repeats the target, W6, and the case W6 was written for kept within 3%, W6b), `tests/unit-analytics.js` (a lighter week read from what the lifts did), `tests/r53.js` (r53's half rule replaced by the one rule, and a part of it read in kilograms), `tests/r24.js` (a swap writes no trimmed mark), `tests/r27.js` (the Guide's words for Last's labels), `tests/r33.js` (its no-gym cases with a second session short of its usual held to r32.1 on the first session alone), `tests/journey.js`, `tests/progress.js` (its model of the rules is the one rule) and `tests/r54.js` (the stall tip's words after 312 A). `tests/migration.js` loads r53's trimmed sessions, saved and in progress, byte for byte, and reads them by the one rule; `tests/seams.js` generates the new fields. `tests/engine.js` calls every adapter, read from `live.ts`, on 1,000 wide histories and six made by hand (a change of units, and three of the coach's lines, which the wide histories may not reach), every module of the engine at its recorded coverage or above. The comparisons of old builds stay pinned to what they proved: `tests/r36.js` (r35 and r52), `tests/r52.js` (r51 and r52) and `tests/r53w.js` (r52 and r53). The sheets and the keyboard are checked with the visual viewport moved as a keyboard moves it; no browser here has an on-screen keyboard, so the real one is V's check on the phone (`OWNER.md`).

### Phase 3b in detail (r52)

Written before any code (V's go, 2026-10-17 20:57: "this needs to work absolutely perfectly seamlessly and not break anything for users"), measured on r51 with `scripts/depmap.js`, the syntax tree and the type checker. This is the third draft: an independent review of each earlier draft found it could not pass its own checks as written, and every finding and what it changed is listed at the end of this section.

**What moves: 120 declarations, about 1,250 lines.** The load engine (`suggest` and everything it calls) and the analytics index (`IDX()`, `dateIndex()`, `pace()` and everything they call), with the readers `tests/r36.js` already holds and the steps 3a left for this phase:

- the engine: `suggest`, `suggestCore` and its cases `sgEasy` to `sgReps`, `sgFirst`, `sgBadDay`, `sgNoStep`, `suggestTimed`, `easierText`, the close-variation start `kinOf` and `kinStart`, steps and racks (`incOf`, `rackStep`, `rackGap`, `rackSnap`, `rackTol`, `gymRack`, `gymStep`, `gymOf`, `barKg`, `exBarKg`, `progStep`, `lighterLoad`, `capOf`, `atCapOf`, `snapUnit`, `swapW`, `roundLoad`), `daysOff`, and the plan a lift is trained at (`planFor`, `defPlan`, `profPlan`, `profRule`, `prof`);
- the index: `IDX` and its six steps, `lastOf`, `statsUpTo`, the RIR reading (`rirEff`, `rirUsual`, `rirEst`, `rirHalfOn`, `calStats`, `calSummary`, `CAL_WEEKS`), hard sets (`isHard`, `hardCredit`), bodyweight (`bwAt`, `bwKnownAt`, `bwRef`, `bwRefLoad`, `bwLoad`, `prLoad`, `loadOf`, `tonnage`), `dateIndex`, `weekSessions`, and `pace` with its time model (`paceRatios`, `sessModelMin`, `dayMinutes`);
- the readers: `avgWeeks`, `avgWeeksTxt`, `actualAvg4`, `firstFullWeek`, `weekStreak`, `weekRoutine`, `weekNeed`, `weekStats`, `stalledIds`, `movers`, `fatigueStalls`, `dropOnlyLift`, `recentLighter`, `regionBalance`, `coach`, `readiness`, `musTrend`, `SIZE_MAP`, `CHK_Q`, `chkWord`, `planHard`, `planCredit`, `planSetsPerWeek`;
- the accessors they share: `EX`, `exName`, `U`, `toD`, `fromD`, `stepRead`, `unitStep`, `fmtW`, `loadLabel`, `activeRoutine`, `sortedSessions`, `injuredSet`, `exInjury`, `freeMode`, `isRestWeek`, `isDeloadWeek`, and the date words `fmtDate`, `fmtWith`, `dtf`, `DTF`;
- the seven caches (`EXMAP`, `_idx`, `_dix`, `_cal`, `_rirUse`, `_bwRef`, `_pace`) and `invalidate`, which clears them.

**What they read and write (measured).** They read the log's `sessions`, `exercises`, `routines`, `activeRoutineId`, `bodyweights`, `cardio`, `injuries` and `deloadWeek`, and of its settings `unit`, `rirMode`, `rirHalf`, `gym`, `barKg`, `profile`, `bands`, `priorities`, `restWeeks` and `todayStart`; the date only through `today()` (22 calls in 16 functions, counted on the syntax tree when built; 21 once the unused one in `suggest` is pruned, and an earlier count of 19 was wrong); the device's language through `Intl.DateTimeFormat` (the dates in the engine's sentences); and nothing else: no `ui`, sync, account, storage or page, no `Date.now`, `Math.random` or `uid`. They write nothing to the log or any record in it and sort no shared list in place. They do write their caches, and two marks on the object `IDX()` returns: `rackStep` keeps rack steps there (`_rack`) and `weekRoutine` the week's routine (`weekRid`); `dtf` keeps date formatters in `DTF`. Nothing in the set calls itself through another except the time model (`dayMinutes`, `pace`, `paceRatios` and `sessModelMin` reach each other), which therefore stays in one module.

**What stays, and when it moves.** `regionSrc` reads a choice kept on the device (`regionWk`), so it moves with storage in phase 4. 306 other legacy declarations read the log, directly or through a moved reader (317 counting the 11 that read only the date), from the readers the screens show (`recent4w`, `prsWithin`, `livePR`, `sessionLength`, `bestSet`, `allTimeBests`, `goalState`, `liftChange`, `plannedSets`) and the planner (`cycleState`, `rankDays`, `rankExercises`) to code that builds markup, reads or writes the session in progress (`greyR`, `whyTarget`, `blockProgress`, the adjusting part `src/17-adjust.js`), writes the log (`fitTargets`), or reaches `ui`, the page or storage. Each moves with the screen that calls it in phase 5 (with storage in phase 4 where it reads storage), in the same form and proved the same way as here, and until then reads the engine through the same adapters the screens use. So the engine and the index are whole after 3b; the analytics as a whole are not, and this plan does not claim it.

**Where each goes.** Every module keeps to its layer, and the import graph has no circle, type-only imports included (`tests/hygiene.js`; the table was checked against the measured dependencies of every declaration):

| Module | Layer | Takes from 3b |
|---|---|---|
| `types.ts` | 0 | the record shapes the engine reads (a session and its sets, a weigh-in, a cardio entry, an injury, the settings it reads), and `Gym`, `DbRack` and `Profile` from `gym.ts` and `profile.ts`, so `model.ts` can name them without importing modules that import it |
| `model.ts` (new) | 1 | `EngineState`, the types of `IDX()`'s result, `Model`, the caches (`freshCaches`), `modelOf`, `invalidateIn`, and the accessors `EX`, `exName`, `U`, `activeRoutine`, `sortedSessions`, `injuredSet`, `exInjury`, `freeMode`, `isRestWeek`, `isDeloadWeek`, `prof`, `rirHalfOn`, `gymOf` |
| `units.ts` | 1 | `toD`, `fromD`, `unitStep`, `stepRead` |
| `routines.ts` | 1 | the plan a lift is trained at, `profRule`, `profPlan`, `defPlan`, `planFor` (one module: `defPlan` calls `profPlan`, which starts from `routines.ts`'s `DEFAULT_PLAN`), and `planHard`, `planCredit`, `planSetsPerWeek` |
| `gym.ts` | 1 | `gymRack`, `gymStep`, `rackTol`, `rackGap`, `capOf`, `atCapOf`, `barKg`, `exBarKg` |
| `format.ts` | 4 to 2 | `DTF`, `dtf`, `fmtWith`, `fmtDate` (the date words, beside the rest) |
| `charts.ts` | 4 to 2 | nothing; `firstFullWeek` reads its `firstDate` |
| `loads.ts` | 2 | `fmtW`, `loadLabel`, `swapW`, `roundLoad` |
| `analytics.ts` (new) | 2 | the RIR reading, hard sets, bodyweight, `IDX` and its steps, `statsUpTo`, `lastOf`, `dateIndex`, `weekSessions` |
| `time.ts` (new) | 2 | `dayMinutes`, `pace`, `paceRatios`, `sessModelMin` |
| `weeks.ts` (new) | 2 | `firstFullWeek`, `avgWeeks`, `actualAvg4`, `avgWeeksTxt`, `weekStats`, `weekRoutine`, `weekNeed`, `weekStreak`, `recentLighter`, `regionBalance` |
| `coach.ts` (new) | 2 | `coach`, `stalledIds`, `movers`, `fatigueStalls`, `dropOnlyLift`, `readiness`, `CHK_Q`, `chkWord`, `musTrend`, `SIZE_MAP` |
| `engine.ts` (new) | 2 | `suggest` and the rest of the engine: `suggestCore`, the cases, `suggestTimed`, `easierText`, `kinOf`, `kinStart`, `incOf`, `rackStep`, `rackSnap`, `snapUnit`, `lighterLoad`, `progStep`, `daysOff` |
| `live.ts` (new) | 3 | the live model and the adapters (below) |

`format.ts` and `charts.ts` move down to layer 2, a change to the layer list and nothing else: both import only `util`, and the engine's sentences use `format.ts`'s `repSpan` and `rangeWords` and the weekly averages `charts.ts`'s `firstDate`, which a layer 2 module cannot import from layer 4. The date words join `format.ts`, so dates are worded in one module. Moving a type between modules changes no code (types leave nothing at run time), and `tests/moved.js` compares code only.

**The model.** `Model` is `{ s, today, c }`: `s` the log (typed as `EngineState`, the fields listed above and no others, so the type checker refuses a read of any other field), `today` a function giving the date the engine reads as today, and `c` the caches under their r51 names, starting as r51's did (`freshCaches()`: every one `null` except `_bwRef`, which is `undefined`, since `bwRef` tests for `undefined` and a `null` there would leave every bodyweight lift without its bodyweight). `modelOf(s, today)` makes one for a test. The types of the cached values (`IDX()`'s result and the records in it) are declared in `model.ts`, since a type the engine infers from a cache it also fills would refer to itself. `src/app/store.ts`'s `State` is typed field by field for the fields the engine reads, as its comment promised for 3b; the rest of it stays open until `normalize()` moves in phase 4.

**Pure functions.** Every moved function that reads the log, the date or a cache, directly or through another, takes the model as its first parameter, always named `M`, and its name gains `In` (`suggestIn(M, exId, plan, opts)`): 96 of them. A function taking the model is known by that first parameter, not by its name (the legacy script already has `loadIn`, `numIn` and `distIn`, which have nothing to do with it). The 16 that read none of it (`dtf`, `fmtWith`, `fmtDate`, `calSummary`, `idxAutoLighter`, `idxLightWeeks`, `lastOf`, `avgWeeksTxt`, `musTrend`, `chkWord`, `sgBadDay`, `sgNoStep`, and the tables `DTF`, `CAL_WEEKS`, `CHK_Q`, `SIZE_MAP`) move unchanged under their own names. `invalidate` becomes `invalidateIn(M)`. Inside a function taking the model exactly five things change, and nothing else may:

1. `state` is `M.s`;
2. `today()` is `M.today()`;
3. a cache `X` is `M.c.X`;
4. a call to a moved reader `f(a, b)` is `fIn(M, a, b)`;
5. the one place a reader is passed as a value, `big.map(exName)` in `coach`, is `big.map(id => exNameIn(M, id))` (`exName` takes one parameter and no moved code reads `arguments`, so the extra index and list `map` passes change nothing).

Types are added, and TypeScript's `noUnusedLocals` refuses nine declarations as written, which are pruned and listed: the seven cases `sgEasy` to `sgReps` each destructure all 31 fields of the context `C` and use a few, `idxLift` destructures `exWin` and does not use it, and `suggest` computes a date `T` it never reads. The unused names and the one unused statement are dropped. `M` is reserved: no r51 declaration in the set and no module uses the name, and in the modules it may be bound only as the first parameter of a function taking the model, never assigned.

**Marks TypeScript cannot check, and where they are counted.** The moved code relies in many places on a fact established in another statement: a list filtered to rated sets is then summed by its RIR (`tr.reduce((a, p) => a + p.rir, 0)`), three sessions all checked for a top RIR are then compared by it. Strict TypeScript cannot follow such a fact and refuses the arithmetic (TS2531), so each place needs a `!` or a cast, or the code would have to change, which a move may not do; a type that pretends the value is never null would be untrue. The review of the second draft counted more than 20 such places by reading; the exact number is the type checker's. These marks are not new escape hatches: each records an assumption the code already made, now visible, with a comment saying where the fact is established. So they are counted apart from the rest: a cast or `!` inside a declaration that a move compares token for token with its baseline counts against that move's own number in this file, recorded when the move is made and only falling afterwards, and every other one counts against the numbers in The outside review, which only fall. `tests/hygiene.js` decides which is which from the baseline itself (a declaration in a module whose name, less the `In` of a function taking the model, was a legacy declaration in r51), so a mark in new or changed code can never ride on a move. The three casts `Object.create(null)` needs (TypeScript types it any; `EX`'s map and two in `rirUsual`) are among them. `Array(n).fill(...)` takes its element type instead (`Array<number>(n)`), which needs no mark and changes no code.

**The live model.** `src/app/live.ts` (layer 3, beside the store) holds the one model the app runs on, `LIVE`: `s` is a getter returning the store's `state` binding, so a log replaced by `setState` is read at once, as now; `today` is `util`'s own `today`, so the date is read at every call, as now (an app resumed the next morning without a reload reads the new date); `c` is its caches, built on first use and cleared by `invalidate`, exactly as r51's. One object, made once: a call through an adapter makes nothing but its list of arguments (the rest parameter, as any call that passes its arguments on does), and reads the clock only when the engine reads the date. `live(f)` turns a pure function into the app's: it returns `(...args) => f(LIVE, ...args)`, so every argument is passed on as before (the four places the legacy script passes a reader as a value, `recentExIds(8).map(EX)` among them, get the same arguments). An adapter has no name and a length of 0, and nothing in the source or the tests reads either. For each of the 74 moved names the legacy script calls (73 readers and `invalidate`), `live.ts` exports `export const X = live(XIn);`, and the legacy script imports `X` from it in `src/09-imports.js`, so not one call in the screens changes; of the 16 unchanged declarations it imports the 8 it uses (`fmtWith`, `fmtDate`, `CAL_WEEKS`, `SIZE_MAP`, `avgWeeksTxt`, `musTrend`, `chkWord`, `CHK_Q`) from their modules.

**Proof, before V sees it:**

1. **The code is r51's.** `tests/moved.js` reads each module's code back before comparing it with r51's, on the syntax tree: a function taking the model loses `In` and its first parameter `M`; a call `fIn(M, ...)`, whose first argument must be `M` itself, is `f(...)`; `M.s` is `state`, `M.today` is `today` and `M.c.X` is `X`; and `id => exNameIn(M, id)` is `exName` only where the reader takes that one parameter. After the read-back no `M` and no reference to a function taking the model may remain, so any other use of the model fails (a nested `M`, `M['s']`, `M.c` or `M` passed whole, a spread, a call without `M`, a reader used as a value). On r51's side the nine listed prunes are applied from a fixed list, each verified: every dropped name is never read in its declaration; `C` is a constant bound to an object of shorthand plain values in `suggestCore`, the only caller of each case in both builds; `trendScope` returns an object literal; and the dropped statement in `suggest` reads `opts.before`, whose callers all pass an object literal, and calls `isDate` and `today`, which change nothing. Then the comparison is token for token, as for every move since r50, for the 96 functions taking the model, the 16 unchanged and `invalidateIn`. What has no r51 counterpart is held to its exact code with an executable check, as `added` entries: `freshCaches` (the seven caches and their starting values, `_bwRef` `undefined`), `modelOf`, `LIVE` (its `s` is the store's log after `setState`, its `today` is `util`'s `today` itself) and `live` (it passes `LIVE` and then every argument and returns what the function returns); every other declaration in `live.ts` must be `export const X = live(XIn);` for exactly the names the legacy script imports, no more and no fewer. The legacy script left behind is r51's, statement for statement, less the moved declarations and the caches (listed as retired, their place taken by `freshCaches`), and less the 22 names added to the test hook (item 3).
2. **Nothing behind the model's back** (`tests/hygiene.js`). Every function taking the model, and every declaration it reaches through any reference (a call, or a function passed as a value such as `.filter(isWork)` or `.sort(sessCmp)`) and import in any module, reads no clock (`today`, `Date.now`, a `new Date()` with no arguments, `performance`), nothing random (`Math.random`, `uid`, `crypto`), no browser global (`window`, `document`, storage, `indexedDB`, `navigator`, `location`, timers, `fetch`, `globalThis`), nothing from `store.ts` or `live.ts`, and no mutable value kept at a module's top level other than `DTF`, a store of date formatters that changes no result: the model is its only source of the log, the date and the caches. `Intl` is allowed and listed, since the engine's sentences word dates in the phone's language. By the review's measurement on r51's code, the 92 module declarations the moved code reaches pass. The check is shown failing on a planted read of each kind, including one reached through a helper in another module and one through a function passed as a value. `scripts/depmap.js` reads a name imported from `live.ts` as a read of shared state, so a screen that calls `EX` is not counted as pure.
3. **The same numbers as r51, on more than r36 reaches** (`tests/r52.js`, new). r51 and r52 side by side in the page. r51's test hook does not reach 22 of the 74 names (`U`, `toD`, `fromD`, `loadLabel`, `exName`, `planCredit`, `gymOf`, `isDeloadWeek`, `freeMode`, `injuredSet`, `prLoad`, `statsUpTo`, `readiness`, `bwRef`, `weekRoutine`, `easierText`, `barKg`, `suggestCore`, `loadOf`, `bwLoad`, `hardCredit`, `rirHalfOn`), so r51 is served with those 22 added to its hook, an edit at that one place with the rest of the page held to the baseline's text, and r52's hook gains the same 22 (a listed rewrite the legacy check reads back). 1,000 seeded histories, built to reach what `tests/r36.js`'s do not: injuries, RIR off and RIR checks, half sets, Your training, a deload week, rest weeks, freestyle and free sessions, check-in answers, sessions marked as demo, priorities and own targets, steps, heaviest loads, kinds and load meanings set on exercises, deleted, archived and custom exercises, a gym with dumbbells (fine steps too), kettlebells, plates and a stack, labelled in either unit, a bar in Settings and on a lift, weekly and cycling routines, superset links, one lift on two days with different ranges, sessions on a routine's days, timed sessions and cardio, and every day of the week as today. Every one of the 74 names is called with arguments covering each form it takes, and the results are compared whole. Identity as well, which a comparison of values cannot see: `EX(id)` returns the log's own record, `prof()` the log's own profile, and `IDX()` the same object until `invalidate`. Scenarios, each run on both builds: the date moved past midnight in a page left open (Playwright's clock), from Sunday into Monday so every week-start reader turns over, read before and after without a reload; each cache read stale on purpose, one at a time (the log changed and read again before `invalidate` runs; an exercise added under a built `EXMAP`; the `_rack` and `weekRid` marks); and `makeDemo`'s whole log (its seed is fixed; ids set aside), since the demo calls the engine while it builds and so depends on when the caches fill.
4. **The engine runs alone** (`tests/engine.js`, new). The engine's modules bundled for Node, with no page: the normalized histories of item 3 (taken from the page, since `normalize()` stays in the legacy script until phase 4) deep-frozen, the language and time zone set to the page's. Each of the 74 on a fresh model gives the page's answer, gives it again on a second fresh model, and writes nothing to the log (a write to a frozen log throws); the functions only the engine calls (`sgEasyIn`, `idxLiftIn` and the like) are reached through them, and how much of each module runs is what the coverage measures. Line coverage of each module of the engine is measured with V8's own coverage and Node's source maps, and recorded below as a ratchet that may only rise.
5. **Everything else.** `tests/r36.js` unchanged (r35 against this build), `tests/coherence.js`, `tests/perf.js` (`live` adds a call and an argument list to the readers the screens call most, about 20 to 30 billionths of a second a call in V8 by the review's measurement and not measured in Safari's engine, so tick, Today, Stats and the rebuild are measured before and after and reported; each stays under its ceiling), and every suite in Chromium and WebKit.
6. Independent reviews before the pull request, each finding closed and replayed to a failure.

**One release or two.** The review offered two releases as the lower risk: the code moved unchanged in the first, still reading the store and the clock as it does now, and given its parameters in the second. What that buys is diagnosis: a fault found in the first would have one suspect, and V's rule is to group only where grouping adds no risk. Against it: an import cannot be assigned to, so while the caches are module variables, all seven, their owners (`EX`, `IDX`, `dateIndex`, `calStats`, `rirUsual`, `bwRef`, `pace`) and `invalidate` would have to share one module in the first release and be split up again in the second; the store would go below the engine in the layers for one release and back after; and every declaration would be moved, typed and proved twice, with a second set of chances to get each wrong. The parts of this release that change what runs are two, each with its own proof: the moved code (read back and compared token for token, item 1) and the live model (exact code and executable checks, item 1, and the identity and scenario checks, item 3); a fault `tests/r52.js` finds names the function it is in. One release, judged the lower total risk; the diagnosis the split would buy is given up knowingly.

**Unchanged by 3b:** what a lifter sees (no number, word or screen), storage keys, `normalize()`, the sync documents, every screen's code. Version 2.3, build r52. One thing differs, found by the reviews and accepted: when code that moved throws, the message the browser writes from the source names the code as written (`IDXIn(...)` where r51 said `IDX(...)` in Chrome's engine, `IDXIn(M)` where it said `IDX()` in Safari's, which writes the arguments too, as the WebKit run on GitHub showed; and `M.s.sessions` where it said `state.sessions`, a log `normalize()` has not checked), and the crash screen shows that message and a problem report its stack, which now has the function taking the model above the adapter. The crash itself, and when it happens, are the same; `tests/r52.js` compares errors by kind and by message with that name read back.

**The reviews of the plan (2026-10-17), and what changed.** The first draft's checks could not pass (`noUnusedLocals` refuses nine declarations as they stand: now pruned and listed; the engine needs `format.ts` and `charts.ts`, both at layer 4: now at layer 2; a model at layer 1 cannot build values made by layer 2: now it holds only the caches, filled by the engine as r51 did; its no-clock rule failed on existing modules and missed `Date.now`, `new Date()`, `Math.random` and `uid`: now it follows every function that takes the model, whatever the module; the parameter name `m` is already used inside twelve declarations, five of which would not parse: now `M`, verified free); it named no rule for a reader passed as a value (now rule 5) or for the caches and `invalidate` (now compared or held to exact code); its "held by a named suite" for the cache owners and adapters reopened the hole r51 closed, where code passed if a suite existed (now exact code and executable checks); nothing tested an app left open past midnight, caches read stale, or the demo, which reads the engine while it builds, and r36's histories never reach injuries, RIR off, half sets, Your training, freestyle, steps, bars, routine days or timed sessions (now `tests/r52.js`); an adapter checked against its own pure function proves nothing, and a second call on one model only reads the cache (now fresh models and the page's answers); `rackStep`, `weekRoutine` and `dtf` write caches, which the draft said nothing in the set does (now said); `easierText` and `invalidate` were missing, the adapters are 74 not about 60, and "neither subsystem is split" overstated it (now the engine and the index are whole, and what stays is counted). The second draft still failed in three places: `routines.ts` and `profile.ts` would import each other (now the plan a lift is trained at is one module, and `Gym`, `DbRack` and `Profile` move to `types.ts`, so no type-only circle forms); strict TypeScript needs a `!` or a cast at more than 20 places where the code relies on a fact from another statement, which the ratchets as written could not admit and no "offset" could pay for (now counted per move, above); and r51's test hook does not reach 22 of the 74 names (now added to both builds' hooks for the comparison). It also found `freshCaches` and `modelOf` held by nothing, where one wrong starting value (`_bwRef` as `null`) would drop bodyweight from every bodyweight lift (now exact code and checks); its reason for one release did not hold, since r51's legacy script already reads the store (now the reason above); gaps in what the histories and scenarios reach (check-ins, demo sessions, deleted and custom exercises, kinds and load meanings, a gym's kettlebells, plates and stack and its own unit, free sessions, weekly routines, superset links, one weekday as today, identity, each cache stale on its own: now all in item 3); the no-clock rule followed calls but not functions passed as values, and did not stop module state (now both); a function taking the model must be known by its parameter, not the suffix `In`; only 8 of the 16 unchanged declarations are imported; and the count of other readers was 306, not 321 (the first count took a use of the unchanged date words or the caches as a read of the log).

**Built (r52), and what building found.** The move is as planned: 120 declarations, 116 statements cut from six legacy parts (10, 15, 16, 19, 20 and 21) with the comments above them, each removed comment checked present in a module by its words, and a section heading left in a legacy part where code stays under it. Comments that explained code which moved went with it: the reasoning for counting RIR 4 to 5 as half a set now sits on `hardCreditIn`, which applies it, and `halfLbl`, which stays, points there; a clause in the muscle scores' comment that named a guard the code no longer has was dropped. The types are in `types.ts` (`EngineState` and the records it holds) and `model.ts`; the store's `State` is `EngineState` plus an open index for the fields the engine does not read; five parameter types of earlier phases were widened for TypeScript's `exactOptionalPropertyTypes` (`loadNote`, `jumpPct`, `setSecs`, `setCredit`, `sessCmp`), which changes no code. Moved code needs 22 casts and 86 `!`, each recording a fact the code already relied on, now counted as the move's own (below); the general counts are unchanged. The index's `byEx` and `exStats` (and, since the second review, every other map of the index and the caches) are typed as possibly absent for a key they do not hold, which is what they hold: typed as always there (the first build), two `!` asserted nothing and the code's own tests for a missing lift read as dead, which the review found; typed truly, each read of an id taken from the index's own keys carries a `!` saying so. Four things in the checks were found while building and fixed: `tests/moved.js` compiled each module with esbuild's ASCII output while the page is bundled in UTF-8, so a `×` was `\xD7` on one side and the minifier named `suggestCore`'s locals differently (modules are now compiled as the page is); `tests/hygiene.js` read the property a destructuring takes (`uid` in `const { uid: _u } = it`) as a value of type any, which the type checker gives such a name (now skipped, its binding still read); the first version of rule 6 gathered its problems and never failed on them, which the planted mutations showed before anything relied on it; and 13 imports in `src/09-imports.js` that only the moved code used were removed. Thirty-eight planted mutations were each caught by the suite meant to catch it before the review (eight more after it, below): 19 for `tests/moved.js` (each use of the model the read-back refuses, a wrong cache, one changed value, a prune dropping a name still read, a misnamed function taking the model, `M` as a module value, adapters wrong or extra, `LIVE` holding the log as a value, `freshCaches` starting `_bwRef` at null, `live` dropping an argument, the hook's names out of place, a changed legacy statement), 12 for `tests/hygiene.js` (the clock read directly, through a helper in another module, randomness through a function passed as a value, `uid`, the store, a page global, `performance.now`, `new Date()`, a module value changed, a new `!` in moved code, a new cast outside it, an any binding still found), 4 for `tests/r52.js` (one value in the lighter day, `invalidate` keeping the bodyweight, the live date read once at load, `EX` handing out copies) and 3 for `tests/engine.js` (the log sorted in place, the clock read behind the model, a module running fewer lines than recorded). `tests/r52.js` compares about 1,630 calls a history over 1,000 histories, 1.63 million in all, every one equal to r51's; `tests/engine.js` makes 1.63 million calls in Node (those before `invalidate`), each on two fresh models of a frozen log, every one equal to the page's. Lines run by them: the engine, the analytics, the model, the time model and the units, all of them; weeks and the coach, all but `avgWeeksTxt` and `musTrend` (not among the 74; `tests/moved.js` holds them token for token), the line for a muscle whose strength is falling and the all-green line; the routines, gym, format and loads modules also hold earlier phases' code the 74 do not reach. `tests/perf.js` after the move, six runs on the same machine: tick 14 to 15 ms (one run 24.5), Today 5 to 8, Stats 28 to 32, rebuild 87 to 103, against 14 to 16, 5 to 7, 27 to 30 and 82 to 104 before. An id such as "constructor" makes `daysOff` throw in both builds (the index's `byEx` is a plain object); no log can hold one, since `okId` turns such an id into a hash when the log is normalized, and `tests/r52.js` calls it only to compare the error.

**The independent review of r52 (2026-10-18)** found no change in any number, word on a normal screen, stored value, cache lifetime, identity or argument, and found the read-back sound. It found: the crash text difference (stated above, under Unchanged by 3b); four checks weaker than they said, each shown passing a plant, each closed and the plant replayed to a failure: `tests/engine.js` ran its bundle in sloppy mode, where a write to a frozen object fails silently, so a property written on the log passed (now strict, and the suite says so); rule 6 missed `Math['random']()`, `Date()`, `new Date(...[])` and a date formatter given no date (now each fails, and `Date` passed as a value too); a cast inside an adapter was counted as moved code's mark, since the compiled code that `tests/moved.js` compares shows no type (each adapter's TypeScript is now held to its exact text); and the comparisons' encoding, `JSON.stringify`, dropped the properties `avgWeeks` keeps out of sight, so a changed `_span` passed (every own property is now encoded, a non-enumerable one marked); the two `!` above; and wrong numbers and words in these documents (19 calls of `today()`, 1,700 calls a history, "no call makes an object", the legacy parts 15 and 16 in CLAUDE.md, item 303's count of functions taking the model) and two stale comments, each corrected. With the review's plants, 46 planted mutations are each caught.

**The second independent review (2026-10-18)**, of those fixes, again found no change a lifter would see, every mark true (each of the 117 in the engine's modules removed in turn left a type error or an any), and the load order safe (nothing run at start reaches a moved value before it exists). It found the fixes incomplete in four places, each shown by a plant every check passed, each closed and the plant replayed to a failure: rule 6 passed `format(undefined)`, a formatter taken as a value, `Object.assign` on a module value, a write through a local alias and a counter kept in a closure made at load (the first four and the closure now fail rule 6; the alias, beyond a reading of the code, now throws in `tests/engine.js`, which freezes every one of the modules' 83 tables while the engine runs); a cast in a statement LIVE shared with an adapter was counted as moved code's mark (code added on purpose must now stand in a statement of its own, and a mark is credited to the declarator that holds it); a write to the log that no answer showed, its error caught, passed `tests/r52.js` and `tests/engine.js` (the digest now compares the log after every call, and `tests/engine.js` watches the log through a proxy on every tenth history's second round, so an attempt is recorded even when its error is caught); and three of `tests/r52.js`'s scenarios still wrote with `JSON.stringify` (now the digest's encoding, the log included). It also found the crash text difference wider than first stated (corrected above), the list of suites skipped in WebKit out of date, `suggest`'s comment silent on seven of its `!`, and the other maps of the index (`weekSets`, `weekConf`, `weekHard`, `dayMus`, `dayMeta`, `lightWk`, the date index and two caches) still typed as always holding a key the code tests for; they are typed truly now, which needed no mark, as the code already tested each. With these, 55 planted mutations are each caught.

**Progress (kept current, so the work survives any break):** built, proved and reviewed twice (2026-10-18); pull request #45. Its first run on GitHub failed in WebKit only, in `tests/r52.js`: Safari's engine words an error with the call's arguments (`IDXIn(M)` for `IDX()`), which the comparison's read-back of error text did not cover; the read-back now follows `tests/moved.js`'s rules, and the second run passed every check in Chromium and WebKit, with the engine's coverage on GitHub equal to the ratchet. The preview is published (build 2026.10.17-r52). V looked at it in the preview (PENDING 309) and merged it on 2026-10-18 at 18:35; published at 18:43, live.

### Phase 3a in detail (r51)

**Part 1: 176 self-contained declarations move** (of the 215 the map calls pure; the other 39 are listed below with the reason each waits). Each goes where it belongs in the finished design, not to the nearest file:

| Module | What it holds |
|---|---|
| `routines.ts` (grows) | routine helpers: a week's training days, superset links and letters, sets a day, a routine's signature, the template count line, the target RIR tip |
| `library.ts` (grows) | exercise search by words, Most common first, its tip |
| `formulas.ts`, `util.ts` (grow) | the load jump tip beside `jumpPct`; the seeded random numbers |
| `profile.ts` | Your training: the choices, focus numbers, `profClean`, its tips |
| `records.ts` | what a stored record may hold: the schema version, limits, id checks, the cleaners for calibration, check-in and targets, session order, the trash limits |
| `gym.ts` | Your gym: plates, dumbbells, `gymClean`, plate modes |
| `chunks.ts` | how the log splits into sync documents |
| `merge.ts` | record fingerprints and the three-way merge, and Undo's merge of a session in progress |
| `session.ts` | a session in progress: grey and effective loads, live targets, the Last column, joining repeated lifts, rest after a block, a blank session, the edit fingerprint, hints said once |
| `loads.ts` | what a load number means, in words |
| `prs.ts`, `trend.ts`, `volume.ts`, `goals.ts` | PR names and bands; a lift's trend window and ends; below and above a target; a goal's gap |
| `importing.ts` | the import parsers: dates, CSV, .xlsx, names, headers, sets typed as text |
| `feedback.ts` | problem report kinds, limits and the screenshot check |
| `layout.ts`, `charts.ts`, `format.ts`, `icons.ts` | sections and their order, accents, sheets with fields; chart windows; dates, durations and counts in words (`estMin` among them); the logo and icons |

Moving into a module that already exists (`routines.ts` and three others) is checked like a new one: `tests/moved.js` is generalized so a module can take declarations from more than one phase, each compared with the baseline of the phase that moved it.

**What waits, and why** (39): the screen markup (`pageHead`, `pickRow`, `tipi`, the body map and its shapes, the safety line, the code field, and the like) moves as components in phase 5, not as strings into a module first; the analytics steps that are pure on their own (`idxLightWeeks`, `calSummary`, `avgWeeksTxt`, `musTrend`, the size map, readiness words) and the engine case `sgBadDay` move with `IDX()` and `suggest` in 3b, so neither subsystem is split across two places; `UNDO_MAX` and `UNDO_TTL` share a statement with the undo store and `logCut` belongs to the log, so they move with storage in phase 4; `EMAIL_RE` moves with the account sheets and `howToUrl` with `howToLink`, its markup; `aiInit` and `aiOn` (with the `AI` object beside them, which is not pure, since one place writes its `ctl` and another reads it) are stubs left by the retired Claude features (r46), which always answer "off": removing them is a change, not a move, so it is listed for V instead of done here.

**Part 2: one owner for the shared state.** `src/app/store.ts` declares `state`, `ui`, `cloud`, `acct` and `acctWait`; the legacy script imports them under the same names, so every reader is unchanged. An import cannot be assigned to, so the 19 places that replace the whole log (`state=normalize(...)`, a restore, a sign-in, Undo) call `setState(...)`, which assigns and returns the value as the assignment did. That is the whole change: `tests/moved.js` gains a check that every statement left in the legacy script is the same as in r50 once `setState(x)` is read back as `state=x`, so nothing else can slip in. The properties of `ui`, `cloud` and the log are still written from many places; giving those writes one path is 3b's and phase 5's work, and this release does not claim it.

**Unchanged by 3a:** storage keys, `normalize()`, the sync documents and their order, every screen. **Proof before V sees it:** `tests/moved.js` (code and names, as in r50, plus the legacy rewrite check), `tests/hygiene.js`, `tests/r36.js`, `tests/coherence.js`, every suite in Chromium and WebKit, and an independent review that has not seen the work. Version 2.2, build r51.

**Built (r51):** 176 declarations moved as listed (one, the .xlsx reader `impXlsx`, is self-contained but uses the browser's DOMParser, so it alone needs a browser to run, while the rest of `importing.ts` loads and runs in Node; the map now counts the web APIs a page provides) and the five shared objects went to `src/app/store.ts` (180 compared with r50, token for token); 18 assignments and the one at start go through `setState`; the 692 statements left in the legacy script are r50's in order once those are read back. Two things changed in the move check along the way: it compares with whitespace minified (esbuild keeps an array's line breaks when it reprints, so a reformatted list read as a change), and code a move adds or changes on purpose (`setState`, `state` declared without a value) carries an executable check in its entry, run against the module itself, since there is no baseline to compare it with. The independent review then found two more holes, each shown passing every check before the fix and failing after: a module that imports another only to run it (`import './x'`), and code added on purpose checked only by its executable check, so `setState` could have cut the log and passed. Now a moved module may not import for effects, every module is in a move or listed as written new, and added or changed code is held to the exact code and names its entry states. Seven comments the move had left beside the wrong code were put back beside theirs (the legacy script's line count rose by 15 for it), `HIST_PAGE` moved to the store so the state layer imports no screen module, and every module is formatted with Prettier from a committed `.prettierrc.json`, checked on every run (at first the r49 and r50 modules kept their hand layout, an exception removed later in r51; the exercise library and the two routine builders (`defaultRoutine` and `TEMPLATES`, functions whose bodies are the tables) keep one record a line behind a `prettier-ignore`, allowed only at a module's top level under a comment saying why, and printed by the check; layout never changes code, which the move check proves). One key in the import parser's name table (`assisted dip`) is written twice with the same value; TypeScript refuses that, so the line carries a marked exception rather than a change, and removing the copy is a change for later. A second independent review, on the fixes, again found no change in behaviour, and four more ways to change it that passed every check, each shown passing before the fix and failing after: code run from `types.ts` or `config.ts`, which no move compared (now `types.ts` must compile to nothing, and `config.ts` to constants made from literals and its own values, read by `tests/moved.js`); a name re-exported under another's name (now a module's name must come from the module that declares it); an import circle between the store and the screen layer that left History's page size `undefined` at start (now the layers and the no-circle rule above); and a copy of the legacy script in a module, read by the move check in place of the real one (now the legacy script is found by the shape `scripts/join.js` gives the bundle, and `@ts-nocheck` and `@ts-ignore` are refused, with the one marked exception listed). `src/09-imports.js` may now hold only named imports from `src/app/` (an empty `import {}` and `export * from` passed the earlier check), and the formatting of the modules from r51 on is checked by Prettier.

A mutation test followed (37 edits that change the built page, each run against the checks): 15 passed every check, from five causes, each now closed and each mutant replayed to a failure. The causes and the rules that answer them: (1) the checks read `src/app/` one level deep, by file name, and matched an import by its base name, so a module in a subfolder under a known name, or a `.tsx` beside a `.ts`, was read by nothing while the bundle took it; now every file under `src/app/` at any depth must be a `.ts` file, a module is named by its path, an import is resolved from the importing file's own place, and `scripts/join.js` pins the extensions the bundler may try. (2) Nothing compared the module code on the page with the modules, so a build script that injected a file, a `package.json` field that swapped a module in, a replace on the bundle's text, a page edited by hand and a second inline script all passed; now `tests/moved.js` checks that `index.html` is exactly what `scripts/join.js` builds, that the script starts strict, that outside the legacy script the page holds only declarations, each one module's by name and code and every module's on the page once, that no two modules declare one name (so the bundler renames nothing), and that the page's other inline scripts are the baseline's; `package.json` may carry no field that maps a module elsewhere. (3) A binding made by destructuring (`const { Math } = ...`, `const [] = [...]`) was not counted as a declaration, so code could run on load or take a global's name; now only plain names pass. (4) The legacy locator took the last script of the legacy shape, so a copy appended after the real one was compared in its place; now there must be exactly one, and `scripts/join.js` refuses legacy parts that are more than the one call. (5) Config, `tsconfig.json` and `scripts/config.js` had no guard: a config value that read a page flag passed with Node seeing the other branch (now only `THREE_URLS` may read `IRONLOG_APP`), a `tsconfig.json` covering one file passed (now its strict settings and its coverage of every module are checked), and a config loader that changed a value passed (now its values must equal the module's). Two checks were over-broad and are not now: a comment that mentions an import or a `@ts-ignore` no longer fails (imports are read from the syntax tree, a directive is a comment that opens with it), and `src/09-imports.js` may import any module that exists, in a subfolder or not.

The release's own review then found five more things, each closed: an assertion (every import used) had been deleted by accident with the formatter block it sat under, and is back; the page around the app script (markup, styles, the loader, any handler attribute) is now held to the baseline's, build id aside, unless a move entry says the release changes the page and why; what `scripts/build.js` adds to the shipped page (the head and boot scripts, the install metadata) is pinned by hash in `tests/dist-page.json`, its app script must be `index.html`'s byte for byte, its account settings `supabase.config.json`'s, and its Content Security Policy must allow exactly its inline scripts (a timer that wiped the log, put in the boot script, had reached `dist/` with every check green); every baseline is pinned by hash in `baselines/SHA256SUMS`, so an edit to one is a visible change to that file; and the workflow runs `tests/hygiene.js` and `tests/moved.js` on their own before the runner, so a runner that dropped one could not hide it.

**A change to the legacy script between phases (until r54; since then legacy code is fixed in place with a failing test first, above).** The statement-for-statement check holds the legacy script to the last move's baseline, so a fix to legacy code is not made by editing it in place: a fix is new code, and new code goes in `src/app/` (rule 9). When a legacy function needs to change, it moves first, as a declaration listed in the move with `changed` (its new code, the names it uses and an executable check), or, for one mechanical rewrite of many declarations, by rules the move lists that read the rewrite back to the baseline's code before the token comparison (3b's form); the legacy copy is deleted in the same change, so the ratchet holds. Code held only by a suite that exists and runs is not enough: the review of 3b's first plan showed that this reopens the hole r51 closed, where `setState` could have cut the log and passed, so every line a move adds is held to exact code (Phase 3b in detail). A change a lifter sees goes through the ordinary suites, `tests/coherence.js` among them, as every release before the move did. Since r53 a declaration already in a module changes after its move through the move check's `edits`: per declaration, its module, the reason, [was, now] pairs of its TypeScript, the names the change newly uses, and an executable check. Each pair is undone in the module's text before the token comparison with the declaration's move baseline; each `now` must be found exactly once in the declaration and once in the file, so a pair cannot reach other code; and the names the declaration uses after the change, less those it used before, must be the entry's list, each bound in the module or a JavaScript global. So every changed line is stated, and everything else in the declaration is still its move's code.

| NEW-40 | Raised 2026-11-02 by V: "Prior exercise seems to repeatedly reopen when logging next time exercise in a freestyle sesh"; V's detail the same day: "The blank / freestyle session was started on today. As I add exercises sometimes filling in the sets of the latest one would take me back to the one prior and have that reopen up" | r67 | FIXED | Not reproduced at first six ways with every set ticked (scratch `free-fold.js`). Reproduced with V's detail (`free-fold3.js`, then `tests/r67.js` section 3): in a blank session started on Today, the first lift left a set short (two of three ticked, the third row empty, as when the app starts a lift at 3 sets and 2 are done); finishing each later lift took the screen back up to it (scroll 855 to 312 px, the earlier lift at the top, open because of its empty row). Cause: a finished exercise brings up the next one not done and, with none below, wrapped round to an earlier one (since r11 at least); a blank session never has one below, so it happened after every lift. Fix (64b4bae, refined by R67-3): a finished exercise brings up the next one not done below it or, with none, the first one above not started, never one started and left; nothing is saved differently and Finish still asks about sets left. `tests/r67.js` section 3 fails on r66 and passes here, with a guard that a routine still brings up the next lift; `tests/personas.js` persona 14 drives it by taps and typing. Told V 2026-11-02 that a different cause, with every set of the earlier lift ticked, has not been seen. |

| RC-1 | Root cause (V, 2026-11-06 20:01: "So when will we address the root causes?"): which loads a lift can use is worked out in eight places (2.27-F12: stepOfIn, unitGridIn, plateGridIn, rackGridIn, gridSnap, plateSnap, rackSnapIn, moveIn). Explains NEW-46, R-2, NEW-9, 387-B1, NEW-69, NEW-70 and the r54 curls. Fix: one function for the loads a lift can use, every reader listed first, then 390 step 1 (kg or lb per lift) built on it | 2.30 and 2.31 | FIXED | 2.31: Your gym's plates (plateSnap) and the step (stepOfIn) read the one list too, so every rule that moves a load (lighter, heavier, the step, the progression step, the rack and plate roundings) asks loadsForIn; plateAt is read only inside it.
